# ===== LM_C4-001-019 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-001-019 — research notes, checked against the corrected transcription (scans 1–19)

Source: the first pass's research notes (answers/unzipped/LM_C4-001-019/LM_C4-001-019_notes.md, §4): R01–R03,
scans 5–9 nos. 1–17, scans 10–14 nos. 1–15, scans 15–19 nos. 1–15, and E01–E04. Each was checked against
LM_C4-001-019_checked.tex. Verdicts are keep, correct (text revised) or drop. Kinds: conjecture (Grothendieck's
conjectures, questions and expectations), unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea. "Unproved" means not proved
at the cited place. The extension group written 𝒢 by the first pass is Grothendieck's 𝔖 (changes, row 2); the notes
below use 𝔖. Totals: 41 kept, 12 corrected, 1 dropped, 7 new.

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 1–4 (contents)

- **R01 — scan 3, §51.11–16 — unpursued implication — keep.** The contents announce the position of the problem
  (11°), splittings (12°), "Caractérisation de la tour de Fermat ; relations de compatibilité, avec M → G_{ρ,σ}" (13°,
  M without hat), generalised Jacobians (14°) and Kummer extensions (15°). None of these arguments is in this package.
- **R02 — scan 4, §52 heading — unpursued implication — correct.** The struck heading reads "Enveloppes schématiques
  des L-groupes et ΠL-groupes", and 1°) reads "Notion de L et ΠL-groupe" (the first pass had "Π-groupes"). The new
  heading, "Approximations de Lie des systèmes de Galois-Teichmüller", keeps the L/ΠL-group notion (1°–2°) and turns it
  towards Lie approximations. The change of heading by itself establishes no equivalence.
- **R03 — scan 4, §53.1 — unproved claim — correct.** The entry reads "Action différentielle et gr. de Teichm. spécial
  (énoncé provisoire)". The group is the "groupe de Teichmüller spécial S𝒯_{1,1} ≃ Gl(2,Z)^∼"; "épicé" was a misreading.
  The statement is marked provisional, and its hypotheses and proof are on pp. 760 ff., outside this package.

### Scans 5–9

1. **Scan 5, "On voit que si S est quasi-compact …" — unproved claim — keep.** The text passes from a fibrewise exponent
   to one n over a quasi-compact base. In char p, fibrewise quasi-unipotence means finite order (scan 5). A uniform n
   needs constructibility from finite presentation, not quasi-compactness alone.
2. **Scan 5, struck "Théorème" — unproved claim (abandoned formulation) — keep.** It is struck and replaced by the
   sufficiency statement "Pour que g soit unipotente, il suffit …". It should not be quoted as a theorem.
3. **Scan 6, "Je me demande si c'est aussi nécessaire, dans de “bons” cas" — conjecture — keep.** The question is whether
   a fibrewise unipotent section is φ(1) for a homomorphism φ: G_a → G over S. E03 answers it negatively for GL₃ over a
   ramified trait.
4. **Scan 6, uniqueness in char 0 and descent — unproved claim — keep.**
5. **Scan 6, x ↦ x^p − x — unpursued implication — keep.** The insertion "peut-être" ("On s'en tirera peut-être en
   appliquant la descente avec des φ qui sont mono") is confirmed on the ink: the remedy is tentative.
6. **Scan 6, "Un sous-groupe de G isomorphe à G_a est-il connu quand on connaît une section partout non triviale ? Est-ce
   du moins vrai sur un corps de base ?" — conjecture — keep.**
7. **Scan 6, the two graphs in G_a² — own idea — keep (high).** Over a field of characteristic p, t ↦ (t, 0) and
   t ↦ (t, t^p − t) are closed immersions G_a → G_a² with the same value (1, 0) at 1. So the answer to both questions of
   note 6 is no. E02 repeats this note and is merged here.
8. **Scan 7, the open of definition — unproved claim — keep.**
9. **Scan 7, reduction to a local base — unproved claim — correct.** The ink reads "quitte à localiser en un pt maximal
   de S∖𝒰, on ~~peut~~ se ramène à examiner le cas où S est local". For S = Spec Z, regularity of dimension 1 then gives a
   trait. Bases that are not regular of dimension 1 need a separate argument.
10. **Scan 8, the schematic closure L "sous-schém. en groupes réduit" — unproved claim — keep.**
11. **Scan 8, "On a gagné si on prouve que L° ≃ G_{aS}" and the doubt about (L_s)° — conjecture — keep.**
12. **Scan 8, φ_s possibly not mono or trivial — conjecture — keep.**
13. **Scan 8, t ↦ πt — own idea — keep (high).**
14. **Scan 9, (8)–(10) — unproved claim — keep.** φ_η extends iff the matrix polynomial Σ Xⁱλⁱ/i! has coefficients in
    End_A(M): a morphism G_a → GL(M) over A is a polynomial matrix with A-coefficients, and φ_η is unique. This takes one
    line but is not written.
15. **Scan 9, retaining the fibrewise hypothesis — unproved claim — correct.** Precisely: over a field of characteristic
    p, g = φ(1) with φ: G_a → G forces g^p = 1, since G_a(k) is killed by p. At a point of residue characteristic 2 the
    hypothesis of scan 5 therefore adds (g − 1)² ≡ 0 modulo the maximal ideal. Note C1 shows that without it "Pour n=3
    idem" is false.
16. **Scan 9, (11) ⇒ (12) ? — conjecture — keep.**
17. **Scan 9, odd and even parts — own idea — correct.** Taking odd and even parts of (9) gives X² ∈ M₄(A) and
    2X + X³/3 ∈ M₄(A). (11) instead writes φ_η(±1) − 1 without its linear term ±X, so (11) follows from (9) only if
    X ∈ M₄(A), which is not assumed (see C3).

### Scans 10–14

1. **Scan 10, (11 bis)–(12 bis) and "Pour en avoir le coeur net …" — conjecture — correct.** The question stands. The
   second coefficient of (12 bis) is written ½ where (12) gives 1/6. This is harmless (C4): with 2 a uniformiser, 3 is a
   unit. Like (11), (11 bis) lacks the linear term (C3).
2. **Scans 10–11, "Théorème (!)", (13) — unproved claim — keep.** As written: G a separated group scheme, locally of
   finite presentation, over a quasi-compact S; g is quasi-unipotent iff g^n = φ(1) for some n > 0 and some φ.
   Sufficiency is called trivial; necessity is examined for S noetherian and integral.
3. **Scan 11, "des dévissages faciles" — unproved claim — keep.**
4. **Scan 11, case a) — unproved claim — keep.** Separatedness makes the equaliser of gⁿ and 1 a closed subscheme. It
   contains η, so it is S because S is integral.
5. **Scans 11–12, the opens U_n and (16) — own idea — keep (high).**
6. **Scan 12, Lemme: "φ_U^n : λ ↦ φ_U(λⁿ)" — unproved claim — keep.** As on scan 11 ("φ_{nη}(λ) = λⁿ = φ_{1η}(nλ)"), the
   λⁿ stands where φ_U(nλ) = φ_U(λ)ⁿ is meant. The text keeps the ink. Note C2 checks the parenthesis "(néc^t.
   unipotente …)".
7. **Scan 12, "à titre de test décisif" — unpursued implication — keep.**
8. **Scan 13, margin "le cas de car. nulle se résoud par l'affirmative à l'aide de la fonction logarithme — posant … =
   Y" — unproved claim — correct (quotation).** For a ℚ-algebra A, X = log(1 + Y) is a polynomial in Y = g − 1 with
   rational coefficients, so X ∈ M_n(A). "Car nulle" of a ring must mean a ℚ-algebra here, as in the main text ("i.e.
   s'il est une ℚ-algèbre").
9. **Scan 13, (20) — conjecture — correct.** The denominator is 3!, not 6!. The letter in "X ∈ M₅(A|K)" remains
   unsettled; if it were A the question would be empty. As written, without ±X, the implication is false; with the
   linear term it follows from the margin (C3).
10. **Scan 13, "il est clair qu'il suffit de prendre pour d une puissance de 2 assez grande" — unproved claim — keep.**
11. **Scan 13, valuation bound for d = pʳ — own idea — keep (high).**
12. **Scan 13, "Corollaire au lemme dubitatif" — conjecture — correct.** The text reads "On doit avoir en effet ceci",
    not "en fait": the corollary is presented as following from the lemma. The exponent of q = p^? and the sign in "de
    car. p >|≥ 0" remain unsettled.
13. **Scan 14, the affine Proposition and (22) — unproved claim — correct.** The power is m = n^ν (resolved). The
    insertion "de t.f.|p.f." and the inserted hypothesis "U schématiquement dense dans S" stay in the statement.
14. **Scan 14, schematic density — own idea — keep (high).** C6 locates where the proof on scan 15 uses it.
15. **Scan 14, u_m and the composite φ_U ∘ u_m — unpursued implication — keep.**

### Scans 15–19

1. **Scan 15, "ce prolongement sera automatiquement un hom de sch. en groupes" — unproved claim — keep.**
2. **Scan 15, (24)–(25) — unpursued implication — keep.**
3. **Scan 15, infinitely generated algebras — own idea — keep (medium).**
4. **Scan 16, Corollaire — unpursued implication — keep, with an addition.** For a homomorphism of groups the constant
   terms a₀ are the coordinates of the unit section, which lie in A. That is why the Proposition needs no condition at 0
   while the Corollary (morphisms of schemes, "il n'est plus question de structure de groupes") needs g_U = φ_U(0) to
   extend.
5. **Scan 16, Remarque — unproved claim — correct.** The reference is "p. 662′|652′" with a prime, not "!". The
   "théorème dubitatif" is the Théorème (!) of 652′ (scan 10). See C5 for what the Proposition actually covers.
6. **Scan 16, §3 opening — unpursued implication — keep.**
7. **Scan 17, "M̲ est représentable … ssi M est libre de type fini" — unproved claim — keep.** True: A ↦ M ⊗ A is
   representable iff M is finitely generated projective, which for a ℤ-module means free of finite type.
8. **Scan 17, margin "il suffit de poser 𝔖̲ = 𝔖_{S₀} ×_{M_{S₀}} M̲_{S₀}" — unproved claim — correct (notation).** The
   group is 𝔖, not 𝒢; the formula is the contracted product along M_{S₀} → M̲_{S₀}. The rest stands.
9. **Scan 17, (26)–(29) — unpursued implication — keep.**
10. **Scan 18, (30) not representable; correct only for Spec A connected — unproved claim — keep.**
11. **Scan 18, M = 0, A = ℤ × ℤ — own idea — keep (high).**
12. **Scan 18, (31), the H-action on M ⊗ A — unpursued implication — keep.**
13. **Scan 19, (33), Zariski sheafification as a limit over partitions — unproved claim — keep.** The limit index
    "i ∈ S|I" is unsettled; the product is over i ∈ I.
14. **Scan 19, Γ(S, M ⊗ O̲_S) — unpursued implication — keep.**
15. **Scan 19, (34), conditions a) b) of §49 — unproved claim — keep.**

### Additional notes of the first pass

- **E01 — scan 5 and scan 9 — own idea — keep (high).** In characteristic p an element in the image of G_a has order
  dividing p. So "unipotent" in the sense of scan 5 is stronger than the usual sense at points of characteristic p.
  C1 and C2 show the consequence.
- **E02 — scan 6 — own idea — drop.** It duplicates note 7 of scans 5–9, which is kept.
- **E03 — scans 6–10, ℤ₂[π], π² = 2, g = I + N, N = E₁₂ + πE₂₃ — own idea — keep (high).** Checked: N² = πE₁₃, and
  log g = N − (1/π)E₁₃. Also g_s = I + E₁₂ = φ_s(1), so g is unipotent in the sense of scan 5, and g² = φ₂(1) with
  φ₂ over A. The example answers the question of scan 6 negatively. It also shows that (9) ⇒ (10) fails for n = 3
  over a ramified trait even with the fibrewise hypothesis.
- **E04 — scan 13, explicit d — own idea — keep (high).**

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **C1 — scan 9, "Pour n=2 c'est trivial, Pour n=3 idem (dans (9) il y a un seul terme avec un dénominateur)" — own
  idea (checks a claim); confidence high.** (9) does not imply (10) for n = 3 over ℤ₍₂₎. The matrix g with ones on and
  above the diagonal has g and g⁻¹ = I − N + N² integral, but log g has the entry ½ in position (1,3). In this example
  g_s has order 4 in characteristic 2, so g is not unipotent in the sense of scan 5. With that hypothesis,
  (g − 1)² ≡ 0 mod 2, the implication holds over an unramified trait: writing X = Y/2, the conditions force
  Y² ∈ 8M₃(A) and then Y ∈ 2M₃(A). E03 shows it fails over a ramified trait.
- **C2 — scan 12, Lemme, "(néc^t. unipotente, d'après des résultats connus généraux de SGA 3)" — unproved claim
  (checked); confidence high.** In the sense defined on scan 5 the extended section need not be unipotent. Take
  S = Spec ℤ₍₂₎, U = Spec ℚ, G = GL₃, φ_U(t) = exp(t log g) with the g of C1: g_s has order 4 and is not φ(1) for any
  φ: G_a → G over a field of characteristic 2. It is unipotent in the usual (SGA 3) sense. The conclusion of the Lemme
  still holds: φ_U(2t) = exp(2t log g) = I + t(2 log g) + 2t²(log g)² has coefficients in ℤ₍₂₎.
- **C3 — scans 9, 10, 13, (11), (11 bis), (20) — own idea; confidence high.** In all three displays the linear term ±X
  of φ_η(±1) − 1 is not written. Read literally, (20) is false: any X with X² = 0 and X ∉ M₅(A) satisfies the
  hypothesis. (11) is not a consequence of (9) unless X ∈ M₄(A). With the linear term restored, the hypothesis is
  φ_η(±1) ∈ M_n(A), and for a ℚ-algebra (20) follows from the logarithm in the margin of scan 13.
- **C4 — scan 10, (12 bis) — own idea; confidence high.** The second condition is written with ½ where (12) gives 1/6.
  2 is assumed to be a uniformiser of A, so 3 is a unit and (1/6)Y ∈ M₄(A) ⇔ (1/2)Y ∈ M₄(A). The written condition is
  therefore equivalent to the intended one.
- **C5 — scan 16, Remarque — unproved claim; confidence medium.** The Remarque says the preceding arguments prove the
  "théorème" of 652′ when S is noetherian of dimension 1. The Proposition (scans 14–15) and its Corollary are proved for
  G affine (of t.f. or p.f.) and U = S∖V(n) schematically dense. The theorem concerns separated G locally of finite
  presentation. So the Remarque covers affine G unless a reduction to the affine case is supplied. One also needs the
  complement of U₁ to lie in V(n) for some n: it is a finite set of closed points when the generic point has
  characteristic 0.
- **C6 — scan 15, proof of the Proposition — own idea; confidence medium.** "mⁱaᵢ ∈ A" is tested inside A_n = A[1/n],
  which presumes A → A[1/n] injective, i.e. n a non-zero-divisor on A. For affine S that is exactly "U schématiquement
  dense dans S", the hypothesis inserted on scan 14. It also gives the uniqueness used in "Pour raisons de densité
  schématique et unicité".
- **C7 — scan 4, §53.5 — unpursued implication; confidence medium.** The corrected entry, "revêtements des germes
  d'espaces, et relations entre germes top. et germes différentiables de surfaces", announces a comparison of
  topological and smooth germs of surfaces. It presumably serves the isomorphism Gl(2,ℤ)^∼ ≃ S𝒯_{1,1} of §53.4. In
  dimension 2 the classical smoothing of surfaces makes the two kinds of germs agree, which is the input one expects
  there. The argument is on pp. 768′ ff., outside this package.


# ===== LM_C4-020-038 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-020-038 (scans 20–38): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each first-pass note is listed with its verdict: keep, correct or drop, with a reason. Notes marked **own (2nd pass)** are added in this pass. Kinds: conjecture/question, unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea, verification. Readings and equation numbers follow `LM_C4-020-038_checked.tex`.

## Scan 20

| Kind | Note | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| unproved claim | Below (35): (34) « se reconstitue à partir de (35) », and Φ induces the isomorphism 𝒢_{S₀}/M_{S₀} ⥲ 𝒢̲/M̲ (= H_{S₀}). The axiomatic description is asserted, not proved, on this page. | keep: unchanged by the corrections. |
| unproved claim | Margin: the hypothesis on H¹ is « entièrement inutile », because the M̲-torsors over S₀ defined by the inverse images of the h ∈ H are « évidemment triviaux ». | correct: the first pass asked for a separate proof, but the proof is immediate (own note below). The note opens « L'hyp. ». |
| own idea | Compare exactness of (37) on sections with the torsor obstruction for an arbitrary S. | correct: superseded by the own note below. Because the torsors are trivial, (37) is exact over every S, not only affine ones. |
| **own (2nd pass)**, verification | For h ∈ H choose g ∈ 𝒢 over h (𝒢 → H is onto). Then Φ(g) ∈ 𝒢̲(S₀) is a section of the fibre of 𝒢̲ → H_{S₀} over h, so that M̲-torsor is trivial. Given a locally constant h: S → H, the pieces where h is constant are open and closed and disjoint, so the lifts Φ(g_h) glue. Hence 𝒢̲(S) → H_{S₀}(S) is onto for every S, and (37) is exact without any H¹ condition, as the margin says. Confidence: high. | new |

The sum is then represented by the disjoint union of copies of M̲. |

| **own (2nd pass)**, unpursued implication | In the usual sense (derived length ≤ 2) the class differs both ways. Every finite group, A₅ for example, is « métabélien » here (the trivial subgroup has finite index). The Baumslag–Solitar group ℤ[1/2] ⋊ ℤ is metabelian in the usual sense but has no finite-index subgroup of finite type that is commutative. So the term means "virtually ℤʳ" (a finite-index subgroup of finite type that is commutative, hence one isomorphic to ℤʳ). Confidence: high. | new |

| **own (2nd pass)**, verification | ⋃_n ℤ[T/n] = ℤ + Tℚ[T]: the coefficient of T^k (k ≥ 1) lies in n^{−k}ℤ, and every rational number is reached for a suitable n, while the constant term stays in ℤ. For A = ℤ + Tℚ[T], Tℚ[T] = p·Tℚ[T], so A/pA = 𝔽_p and every closed fibre is a reduced point (trivial group), while A ⊗ ℚ = ℚ[T] gives 𝔾_{a,ℚ}. | **own (2nd pass)**, verification | (56) describes Hom_ℤ(ℚ, A): an additive f: ℚ → A gives x_n = f(1/n) with m·x_{mn} = x_n, and conversely, since ℚ is the colimit of the (1/n)ℤ. Hence ℰ¹(A) ≅ A for every ℚ-algebra, and ℰ¹(A) = 0 when nA = 0 (f(1/k) = n·f(1/(nk)) = 0). The text writes this trivial group as « {1} ». Confidence: high. | new |
| **own (2nd pass)**, claim checked | « il existe sans doute un tel n »: if every prime ideal of A has residue characteristic > 0, then Spec(A ⊗ ℚ) is empty, so 1 ⊗ 1 = 0 and n·1 = 0 for some n ≥ 1. The claim holds when the residue characteristics are taken at all points of Spec A. It fails if only maximal ideals are meant (A = ℤ_(p)). « ce qui signifie que les car. résiduelles de A divisent n » is an equivalence only up to a power of n: A = ℤ/p², n = p. Confidence: high. | new |
| **own (2nd pass)**, refutation | « si par contre A de car 0, on a ℰ¹(A) ≃ A » is false as written: A = ℤ has characteristic 0 but ℰ¹(ℤ) = Hom(ℚ, ℤ) = 0. Equivalently, a ring map ℤ + Tℚ[T] → ℤ must send T to an integer divisible by every n, hence to 0. The statement holds for ℚ-algebras, for instance fields of characteristic 0. Confidence: high. | new |

# ===== LM_C4-039-057 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-039-057: research notes checked against the corrected transcription (scans 39–57)

**Source:** the first pass's research notes R01–R51 in `answers/unzipped/LM_C4-039-057/LM_C4-039-057_notes.md` (§5).
Each note was checked against `LM_C4-039-057_checked.tex`. "Row n" refers to `LM_C4-039-057_changes.md`.

**Verdicts:**
- keep: the note stands as written.
- correct: the note is revised; the reason is given.
- drop: the note is withdrawn.

**Kinds:**
- conjecture: Grothendieck's conjectures, questions, expectations and programme.
- unproved claim: a claim without proof in the cited passage.
- unpursued implication.
- own idea.

Notes marked **Claude (second pass)** are new.

These pages are §51 "Exemple: la tour de Fermat": the end of the first construction of Z̃_{ρ,σ} ("version
provisoire", upper pp. 667–672) and "3. Les groupes 𝕆_N^*" (pp. 673–676). Notation used below: M̲ has basis λ₀, λ₁,
ρ_M = [[0,−1],[1,−1]], σ_M = [[0,1],[1,0]], τ_M = −1 (30).

## Notes of the first pass

### Scans 39–41

- **R01 — scan 39 — unpursued implication — keep.** "localement" is now read in full in a) as well as in b) (row 3).
  The question of which topology "localement" refers to stands.
- **R02 — scan 39 — own idea — keep.** Checked on the ink (c039_2_eta): the middle member is β₀σ(β₀)ω^{ν₂}, with no
  inverse, while (25) defines η₀ = β₀σ(β₀)⁻¹. This is a slip in the display, not a different definition.
- **R03 — scan 40 — unpursued implication — keep.** The computation: 1−ρ_M has determinant 3, so its image has index 3.
  1−σ_M has rank 1, with image Z(λ₀−λ₁) and kernel Z(λ₀+λ₁). That agrees with the corrected wording "partout (en tte
  caractéristique) pour le second".
- **R04 — scan 40 — conjecture — keep.** "(si possible…)" is not answered by a proof in these pages. Scan 48 asserts the
  group structure by appeal to "calculs déjà faits trente six fois" and to schematic density.
- **R05 — scan 41 — conjecture — correct.** The margin of the same page limits the hope: "Mais ce n'est le cas qu'en
  car. ≠ 3 !" (rows 6–7). Scan 49 then finds that the relative dimension of Z̃^{+00} jumps from 2 to 3 in
  characteristic 3. So "semble bien lisse" can hold only over Z[1/3].
- **R06 — scan 41 — own idea — correct (settled).** In the bases λ₀, λ₁ the map (α₀,β₀) ↦ (1−ρ)α₀ − (1−σ)β₀ has matrix
  [[1,1,−1,1],[−1,2,1,−1]]. Its elementary divisors are 1 and 3, its image is {c₀λ₀+c₁λ₁ | c₀+c₁ ≡ 0 (3)}, as in (62),
  and its cokernel is Z/3Z. This is the author's "car. ≠ 3".
- **R07 — scan 41 — unproved claim — keep.** The deferred presentation stands. "normalisateur" is now settled (row 8).
  The two superscripts differ: a crossed # on L₀, and a zigzag (♮?) on M_{ρ,σ} (row 9).

### Scans 42–44

- **R08 — scan 42 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R09 — scan 42 — own idea — correct.** The + signs in (33) and (35) are clear in the ink. The overwritten signs on
  scans 41 and 47 belong to two separate changes of convention, analysed in N1 (ν₂: a reparametrisation) and N2 (ν₃: a
  real difference modulo 3).
- **R10 — scan 43 — unproved claim — keep.** The author's own "vaseux" stands. The later qualifier "au moins là où ils
  ont un sens" (row 14) shows that he limits the retroactive justification himself.
- **R11 — scan 43 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R12 — scan 44 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R13 — scan 44 — unproved claim — keep.**

### Scans 45–47

- **R14 — scan 45 — unproved claim — correct.** The reduction is sound. By (44), λ_∞ commutes with σ; it commutes with
  ω (central) and with η₀ ∈ M (M abelian). So once (49) gives λ'_∞ = μλ_∞, the relation [σ',λ'_∞] = 1 follows. The
  remaining relations are (47), which gives (48).
- **R15 — scan 45 — unproved claim — correct.** The qualifier reads "(au moins là où ils ont un sens, i.e., pour u_𝔊
  et λ ∈ M)" (row 14), not "car maintenant". The author restricts the retroactive justification to u on M; the check
  asked for in R15 stands within that scope.
- **R16 — scan 46 — unproved claim — correct (verified).** ρ_M has characteristic polynomial x²+x+1 (trace −1,
  determinant 1), so 1+ρ_M+ρ_M² = 0; and (1+σ_M)(1−σ_M) = 1−σ_M² = 0. Hence (50) holds for every ζ ∈ M̲, ν₃λ₁
  included. "triviale" is correct.
- **R17 — scan 46 — own idea — keep.** A reformulation only; R16 settles the case at hand.
- **R18 — scan 47 — unproved claim — correct (verified).** ζ, η₀ ∈ M act trivially on the abelian M, so conjugation by
  ζρ and by η₀ω^{ν₂}σ induces ρ_M and σ_M on M: ρ'_M = ρ_M and σ'_M = σ_M. μ·id commutes with both, so "également
  évident" holds.
- **R19 — scan 47 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R20 — scan 47 — own idea — correct.** The formula in (51) reads literally μ = 4ν'+2+ν₂ (row 16). It is a slip for
  4ν'+2ν₂+1 (scans 42–43), or for 4ν'−2ν₂+1 in the convention of scans 50–51 (N1). Fibres should be computed from the
  intended formula, and the slip should be recorded as such.

### Scans 48–50

- **R21 — scan 48 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R22 — scan 48 — unproved claim — keep.** The middle term of (54) is Z̃_{ρ,σ} (row 17), so (54) is the kernel
  sequence of the map in (53). The margin's "n'est pas un épi … canular en car. 3" locates the failure of
  surjectivity at 3. It is cured by the auxiliary group of scan 51 (N5).
- **R23 — scan 48 — own idea — correct.** The "+" in "(1+ρ_M)α₀" of (55) is in the ink (c048_2_eq55). But with
  ν₂ = ν₃ = 0 and μ = 1, the loop equation (32) gives (1−ρ_M)α₀ − (1−σ_M)β₀ = 2ν'λ₁ with 4ν' = 0, and (57) and (59)
  have 1−ρ_M. The + is a slip.
- **R24 — scan 49 — unproved claim — keep, with the computation.** Modulo 3 the matrix of R06 has rank 1, so the kernel
  scheme has fibre dimension 3 at p = 3 and 2 elsewhere. It is not flat over Z_(3), and (58) Z̃^{+00} ≃ V(P̲) can hold
  only over Z[1/3]. This is the margin's "sa dimension relative saute de 2 à 3" and the inserted "en car. ≠ 3".
- **R25 — scan 49 — own idea — correct (settled; see R06).** The elementary divisors are 1 and 3. The "= 3a₁" under
  c₀+c₁ in (62) holds only with b₁−b₀ in the first group of (61): with + one gets 3a₁+2b₀. This favours "−" for the
  overwritten sign, which stays marked unsure.
- **R26 — scan 50 — unproved claim — keep.** The calculation is right given 2ν'−ν₂+ν₃ ≡ 0 (3) and μ = 4ν'−2ν₂+1. That
  congruence uses the ν₃ sign of (28)/(29) (N2).
- **R27 — scan 50 — conjecture — correct.** The proposal is struck: two diagonal strokes cross the rest of the page.
  It is carried out on scan 51 as the auxiliary group scheme defined by (63).
- **R28 — scan 50 — own idea — keep.**

### Scans 51–53

- **R29 — scan 51 — unproved claim — keep.** The heading is "3. Les groupes 𝕆_N^*" (row 19).
- **R30 — scan 51 — unproved claim — keep.** See N3: (67) b), as written, is not compatible with (66).
- **R31 — scan 51 — own idea — keep.**
- **R32 — scan 52 — unproved claim — keep.** The last term of (68) is confirmed as λλ', with no N (row 21).
- **R33 — scan 52 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R34 — scan 52 — own idea — correct (confirmed).** With J = NZ, the ½-direct law on A_{S₀}·J̲ (70) gives
  N[λ]·N[λ'] = N[Nλλ']. So (68) should end in N[nλ'+n'λ+Nλλ']; the ink has λλ' (c052_1_eq68). The image in G_a
  confirms this: (n+Nλ)(n'+Nλ') = nn' + N(nλ'+n'λ+Nλλ'). Consequences: R39.
- **R35 — scan 53 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R36 — scan 53 — unpursued implication — keep.**

### Scans 54–57

- **R37 — scan 54 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R38 — scan 54 — unproved claim — keep, checked.** At p | N every section n+N[λ] of 𝕆_N maps to the constant n in
  G_a, so the image of 𝕆_N^* in G_m is a finite constant group (not an epimorphism there), and the kernel is the whole
  fibre G_a (not a monomorphism). Both claims hold for N ≠ 1.
- **R39 — scan 54 — unproved claim — correct.** With the law corrected as in R34, the set (77)
  {1+N[λ] | Nλ = 0} is _N G_a as a group scheme too: (1+N[λ])(1+N[λ']) = 1+N[λ+λ'+Nλλ'] = 1+N[λ+λ']. The parenthesis
  "(sans structure de groupe)" is therefore more cautious than necessary. Scan 55 indeed goes on "qui est un schéma en
  groupes". But (77) is the kernel of (76) only over the component n ≡ 1 (N); see N6.
- **R40 — scan 55 — unproved claim — keep (verified).** _N G_a = Spec Z[x]/(Nx) is the zero scheme over Z[1/N], has
  fibre A¹ at p | N, and is not flat for N ≠ 1. This describes (77), not the whole kernel of (76) (N6).
- **R41 — scan 55 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R42 — scan 55 — own idea — keep.**
- **R43 — scan 56 — own idea — keep (verified on the ink and by arithmetic).** 5 ≡ 1 (4) and 5 ≡ −1 (6), so +5 has
  ε₂ = +1, ε₃ = −1 (ν₂ = 0, ν₃ = 1), as (83) gives. The table puts +5 under ν₂ = 1, ν₃ = 0, and −5 (≡ 7) under ν₂ = 0,
  ν₃ = 1: the off-diagonal entries are interchanged with respect to their own labels.
- **R44 — scan 56 — unproved claim — keep.**
- **R45 — scan 56 — own idea — keep.** μ₀ − μ'₀ = −24ν₂ν₃ = 12·(−2ν₂ν₃), as stated.
- **R46 — scan 56 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R47 — scan 57 — own idea — correct (quantified).** With − before 3ν₂ν₃, (86) and (87) both expand to
  μ = 1+4ν₃−6ν₂−12ν₂ν₃+12ν₀, that is, to 12(ν₀−ν₂ν₃) instead of the 12(ν₀−2ν₂ν₃) of the first display and (89).
  The difference, 12ν₂ν₃, vanishes mod 4 and mod 6, so the congruences hold; but the brackets that define ν' and ν''
  are off by 3ν₂ν₃ and 2ν₂ν₃. The two-row check after (87) agrees with itself when the sign is −.
- **R48 — scan 57 — unpursued implication — keep.**
- **R49 — scan 57 — unproved claim — correct (verified).** (1+NZ)^* = {λ ∈ G_a | 1+Nλ invertible} is open in G_a, so
  smooth of relative dimension 1. By (74), 𝕆_N^* is an extension of the finite étale (Z/NZ)^*_{S₀} by it, so 𝕆_12^*
  is smooth over Z. The inserted "le schéma en groupe lisse" is right.
- **R50 — scan 57 — unproved claim — correct (verified).** A section n+12[λ] of 𝕆_12^* has n ≡ μ₀ (12) for exactly one
  (ν₂,ν₃). The identification of (66) rewrites it as μ₀+12[ν₀] with ν₀ = λ+(n−μ₀)/12, which is unique. Uniqueness holds
  in 𝕆_12^*, not after passing to G_m, where 12ν₀ is lost at 2 and 3.
- **R51 — scan 57 — own idea — keep.**

## New notes — Claude (second pass)

- **N1 — scans 41–51 — own idea.** The ν₂ convention changes. Scans 42–43 have μ = 2ν+1 = 4ν'+2ν₂+1, and (51) has the
  slip 4ν'+2+ν₂. Scans 50–51 have μ = 4ν'−2ν₂+1, in (63) and the scan-50 margin, and the signs in ν = 2ν' ∓ ν₂ on
  scans 41 and 47 are overwritten. Because ν₂ is idempotent, 4ν'+2ν₂ = 4(ν'+ν₂) − 2ν₂: the two conventions differ by
  the reparametrisation ν' ↦ ν'+ν₂ and give the same μ and the same μ ≡ 1−2ν₂ (4).
- **N2 — scans 39–50 — own idea.** The ν₃ sign is not a mere reparametrisation. (28) and (29) put ζ = α₀−ρ(α₀)−λ₁ for
  ν₃ = 1, and scan 50's 2ν'−ν₂+ν₃ ≡ 0 (3) follows from that sign. (33), (35) and (45) write +ν₃λ₁, and scan 47's
  "vrai par hypothèse (cf (31))" needs + in (31). Since 2λ₁ ∉ Im(1−ρ_M), the two signs give different conditions
  mod 3. The overwritten signs in (31) and (32) record the hesitation and stay unresolved.
- **N3 — scan 51 — unproved claim (false as written).** (67) b) states n+N[λ] = n'+N[λ'] ⟺ n+Nλ = n'+Nλ' in ΓG_a.
  Under the contracted product (66), n+N[λ] equals (n+Nk)+N[λ−k] only for locally constant integers k. Equal images in
  G_a therefore do not give equal sections: over Q with N = 2, 0+2[0] and 1+2[−1/2] both map to 0 but are distinct.
  The map to (Z/NZ)^* in (74) and the kernel _N G_a on scans 54–55 both rest on the finer relation; read literally,
  (67) b) would make 𝕆_N → G_a a monomorphism.
- **N4 — scan 45 — own idea (check).** The struck identification 𝔊₀⁺ ≃ SL(2,Z) = {ρ,σ | ρ⁶ = σ⁴ = 1, ρ³ = σ²} is
  correct: SL(2,Z) ≅ Z/4 *_{Z/2} Z/6, with σ = S of order 4 and ρ = ST of order 6. So (48) is the universal property
  of this amalgam, and 𝔊⁺ is its quotient by the normal subgroup generated by [σ,λ_∞].
- **N5 — scans 48–51 — unpursued implication.** The loop equation (1−ρ_M)α₀ − (1−σ_M)β₀ = cλ₁ can be solved locally
  exactly when c ∈ 3·O_S (R06). The struck revision on scan 50 replaces c = 2ν'−ν₂+ν₃ by 3ν'' with
  2ν'−ν₂ = 3ν''−ν₃, and so makes the parameter map onto. This removes the char-3 non-surjectivity that the scan-48
  margin calls "un peu cavalier" and "canular en car. 3". It is the motive for the auxiliary 𝕆_N^* construction that
  follows.
- **N6 — scans 54–55 — unproved claim (incomplete).** "Son noyau est isomorphe au syst. des (77)" covers only the
  sections with n ≡ 1 (N). A section n+N[λ] with n ≢ 1 and n+Nλ = 1 also lies in the kernel of (76): over Q with
  N = 3, 2+3[−1/3] maps to 1. Over Z[1/N] the kernel of (76) is the constant group (Z/NZ)^* (for N = 12, μ×μ), not
  zero. Exactly (77) ≅ _N G_a is the kernel of 𝕆_N^* → G_m × (Z/NZ)^*_{S₀}, the map that (79)–(81) and the Proposition
  (88) go on to use: the "maladresse" of scan 55 in another guise.

# ===== LM_C4-077-095 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-077-095 (scans 77–95): research notes, checked

The first pass's research notes, each checked against the corrected transcription `LM_C4-077-095_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped with a reason. The notes use the corrected readings: the quotient group is 𝕆₁₂* (the author's O*_N), the exponent of N̲_ρ is « ? », distinct from the « ! » of Z̲_ρ^!, and the angular exponent is written ♮. Notes labelled « Claude » are new.

## First-pass notes, scans 77–81

1. **Scan 77, implication not pursued — keep.** The author checks that N̲_ρ → 𝕆₁₂* is epi by giving, for each local section μ̲, the element v = σ^{ν₃}δ^{−ν″}u_μ̲ ∈ ΓN̲_ρ with χ̲(v) = μ̲ ((154)). This is a local lift, not a homomorphic section; the page does not check how these lifts multiply.

2. **Scan 78, claim without proof — correct.** The ink reads « nul au dessus de Spec ℤ ∖ {3}, mais de dim 1 en dim 3 », a slip for « en car. 3 ». If ρ_M̲ acts on the rank-2 module M̲ with characteristic polynomial X²+X+1, then det(1−ρ_M̲) = 3. Hence 1−ρ_M̲ is injective where 3 is invertible, and its kernel is a line over 𝔽₃. That argument justifies the claim, but it is not on the page.

3. **Scan 78, expectation (« la "bonne" définition … », (159)) — keep, reading corrected.** The equation is « ℓ|ε (1−ρ_M̲)x = 0 dans M̲₀ ». The sign before the bracket is €-shaped and unresolved. The comparison with the schematic closure of scan 79 stands.

4. **Scan 79, claim without proof (the closure fits into 1 → L̲_ρ → N̲_ρ → 𝕆₁₂* → 1) — keep.** No change of reading affects it.

5. **Scans 79–80, implication not pursued ((162)–(163)) — keep.** The containment is proved on scan 80 by reducing mod M̲ (ρ = ρ^{ε₃} in H⁺, hence ε₃ = 1). Only the scheme-theoretic hypotheses for « section partielle » remain implicit.

6. **Scan 80, question (« Est-ce que τ′ normalise Z̲_ρ^♮ ») — keep.** Scan 81 answers it in the negative and passes to the ε₂-subgroups.

7. **Scan 81, claim without proof (Proposition, Corollaires 1–2) — correct.** The criterion reads « ssi μ̲(x) est une section de 𝕆₁₂*″ », with μ̲(x) the 𝕆₁₂*-component of x, not χ(x). In « égale à ν₂ (x) » the χ is struck, and (165) writes ε₂(x) directly. The computation σu_μ̲σ⁻¹u_μ̲⁻¹ = ω^{ν₂} supports the Proposition; the passage to equalities of subgroup schemes is not written.

8. **Scan 81, question in the margin — correct.** The note reads « A-t-on Z̲_ρ″^♮ = (N̲_ρ)⁰ (comp. neutre) ? Il faudrait aussi [relations, struck] G̲⁰_ρ,σ, G̲_ρ,σ/G̲⁰_ρ,σ … comparer avec les diagrammes (38) p. 622′ ». So besides the question there is a stated programme: neutral components and component groups of G̲_ρ,σ, compared with diagram (38). The first pass's separate oblique note « Je préfère|Je trouve » is the « Il faudrait » of this note.

9. **Scan 81, claim without proof (Corollaire 3) — correct.** The ♮ on N‴_{ρ(−j̄)} is cancelled in both (168) and (169). The corollary therefore concerns N‴_{ρ(−j̄)} = {1, τ′}_{S₀}·Z̲_ρ^{0♮} ⊂ N̲_ρ, which χ_ρ maps isomorphically onto 𝕆₁₂*‴ = {(ε₂ε₃)(μ̲) = 1}. That it is a subgroup, that the map is injective, and what its image is are all asserted, not proved.

10. **Scans 78–81, own idea of the first pass (base change at the prime 3) — keep.** The schematic closure of scan 79 and the « dim 1 en car. 3 » fibre make the comparison of neutral components before and after reduction at 3 a natural check.

That it is a splitting needs μ̲ ↦ u_μ̲ to be a homomorphism, which the semidirect decomposition (150)–(151) is taken to supply.

## Claude's notes (new)

C1. **Scans 77, 82, 94 — implication not pursued.** (154) gives the lift v = σ^{ν₃}δ^{−ν″}u_μ̲, and the repeated (212) gives the fibre of N̲_ρ^? over μ̲ as {ω^i v | i ∈ Γ{0,1}_{S₀}}. The ambiguity ω^i is therefore exactly the kernel μ_ρ = {1, ω}_{S₀} of (172). The page does not state this check of the bi-section claim, which is immediate from the two formulas.

C2. **Scan 78 (158) — Claude's observation.** The condition ρ_M̲x = x is equivalent to (1−ρ_M̲)x = 0, but the ink's sign after « (1 » is a hooked stroke, not a clean minus. If it is a plus, it is a slip; nothing later depends on it, since (159) writes (1−ρ_M̲).

C3. **Scan 81 — Claude's observation.** The Proposition's criterion in terms of μ̲(x) agrees with (154), where χ̲(v) = μ̲. For x ∈ ΓG̲^♮_ρ,σ, μ̲(x) is χ̲(x), so the change of letter does not change the statement.

C4. **Scans 78–79 — question.** The kernel Z̲_ρ^0 = Z̲_ρ ∩ M̲ of (158) has a fibre of dimension 1 at 3. The improved N̲_ρ is the schematic closure of the part over S₀ − {3}. The page leaves open whether this closure is flat over ℤ₍₃₎ and what its fibre at 3 is: it states only the exact sequence (159) with kernel L̲_ρ.

C5. **Scans 84 and 89 — Claude's observation.** The signs joining 𝔾_a with SZ̲_σ^♮ (scan 84) and, probably, M̲(½)/μ_ρ with 𝕆₁₂*·𝔾_a ((204), scan 89) are vertical ≃, not equalities. The identification with 𝔾_a depends on the chosen generator λ₀+λ₁ of the σ-fixed line (178), so it is canonical only up to sign. The action through χ in (181) is the scalar action on that line.

C6. **Scan 89 (204) — question.** The graph λ ↦ (λ, λ(λ₁+λ₂)) puts the M̲-coordinate on the line spanned by λ₁+λ₂. But (178) gives the σ-fixed line SZ̲_σ^♮ = {a(λ₀+λ₁)}, and M̲(½) is cut out by U = b with b ∈ N̲_σ^♮. Unless the coordinates of (204) are permuted relative to (178), one expects λ₀+λ₁. The ink reads λ₁+λ₂ in both places on the page; check against the definitions of λ₀, λ₁, λ₂ and of σ_M̲ in (150)–(151).

C7. **Scan 91 — question.** The group in « d'indice 2 dans Γ̲′_ρ,σ » carries a prime but no tilde. The second (209) defines Γ′~ = Ker(Γ̲~ → μ) through ε₂, so index 2 would be expected in Γ̲~_ρ,σ. Either the prime marks a different group or the tilde was left out.

C8. **Scans 92 and 95 — implication pursued later.** On scan 92 the author proposes to replace the ~ of Γ_ℚ~ by « ét » (« étendu ») and writes M = N_{1,1}~. In (217) on scan 95 he already writes M = N_{1,1}^{ét}: the proposed notation is adopted three pages later.

C9. **Scan 95 (216) — Claude's observation.** Z_ρ,σ = Ẑ*·L̂₀ with L̂₀ ≃ Ẑ·λ₀ uses the line of λ₀, the completed L₀^M̲ = Ker(Z̲_ρ,σ → 𝕆₁₂*) of scan 85. SZ̲_σ^♮ ≃ 𝔾_a uses the line of λ₀+λ₁. These are two different 𝔾_a-factors, which the page does not relate.


# ===== LM_C4-096-114 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-096-114 (scans 96–114): research notes, checked against the corrected text

Each of the first pass's 57 notes is listed with its verdict: keep, correct or drop, with the reason. Notes marked **own (2nd pass)** are added in this pass. The kinds are conjecture/question, unproved claim, unpursued implication, own idea and verification. Readings and equation numbers follow `LM_C4-096-114_checked.tex`. In particular, the unit group is 𝕆₁₂* throughout (formerly read « Φ₁₂*/𝔻₁₂* »).

| **own (2nd pass)**, verification | Take a point of X_n with x₀ = 0. Then x₁, x_∞ ≠ 0, the element (ζ,1,1) fixes the point, and it is non-trivial modulo the diagonal μ_n for ζ ≠ 1, so the action on X_n is not free. On U_n, (ζ₀,ζ₁,ζ_∞) fixes a point exactly when ζ₀ = ζ₁ = ζ_∞, so F_n° acts freely. This is why the cancellation and the restatement for U_n are needed. Confidence: high. | new |

In the coordinates (α₁,β) on K the images are (−1,−1) and (2,1), with determinant 1. So M → K is an isomorphism of ℤ-modules, as the proposition needs. Confidence: high. | new |

# ===== LM_C4-115-133 (checked notes) =====

# LM_C4-115-133 (scans 115–133): research notes, checked against the corrected transcription

Each of the 40 first-pass research notes (R01–R40, in the first pass's order) is checked against
`LM_C4-115-133_checked.tex` and kept, corrected or dropped, with the reason. The notes added in the second pass are
S01–S08 ("second pass, own"). The kinds are: conjecture/question, claim without proof, implication not pursued, own
idea. The readings used below are those of the checked text: 𝔖 (not 𝒢), 𝕆₁₂* (not Φ₁₂*), M̃ = 𝓜/[Π,Π] written as
script 𝓜 with a tilde, and γ_M(λ) in (280).

## First-pass notes

**R01 — scan 115 — claim without proof — CORRECT.** The first pass treats « la condition sur ζ est automatiquement
satisfaite » as resting on equations outside the packet. The reason has in fact already been given: in (245) of the
preceding package (scan 106) the author shows 1 + ρ_M + ρ_M² = 0 on M̲ (« cette condition est tjrs satisfaite »). The
statement is a reference to a proved identity. The condition on η reduces to (247), η = bδ with b ∈ Γ𝔾_a (the ink has
no S₀).

**R02 — scan 116 — claim without proof — KEEP, sharpened.** The Proposition over any base S rests on (253)–(255) of
the preceding package. There the splittings form a torsor under K̲ ≃ ℤ², and the conjugation map M̲ → K̲ is the
isomorphism (254). The uniqueness of the conjugating section is the injectivity of that map; S01 checks it over ℤ.

**R03 — scan 117 — claim without proof (reference) — KEEP.** « le calcul p. 703 s'appliquant ne varietur » refers to
(260) in the preceding package. The only new ingredient is the central factor ω^{ν₂} in the commutation with σ′,
which yields (270), ν₂(μ̲) = 0.

**R04 — scan 118 — claim without proof — KEEP, completed.** The page's reason for « n'est scindée sur aucune base S
non vide » is (270)–(271). The centraliser T̲ of a section subgroup maps onto 𝕆₁₂*'' only, because above μ̲ with
ν₂(μ̲) ≠ 0 the equation keeps the factor ω, a nowhere-trivial section of the constant group {1,ω}_{S₀}. A splitting
over 𝕆₁₂*×H̲⁺ would need such elements. The argument needs ν₂ to be non-zero on 𝕆₁₂*(S) for every non-empty S, as
for a constant group; the page does not say this.

**R05 — scan 119 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** Comparing another solution of (273) with the reference
splitting a₁ = b = 0 should reproduce the conjugation by a unique section of M̲ that the Proposition on scan 116
asserts.

**R06 — scans 118–119 — own idea — KEEP.** Testing the obstruction and conjugation equations on finite quotients of
M remains a reasonable check; on its own it proves nothing about the profinite statement.

**R07 — scan 120 — claim without proof — CORRECT.** The deduction of (277) is one line. From (274), (276) and
u′(λ) = μλ, the relation λ₀^{μ}·u′(ρ) = u′(ρ′) = ρ′ = λ₀ρ gives u′(ρ) = λ₀^{1−μ}ρ (M written multiplicatively). So
(277) is this formula together with μ − 1 = 2ν, a relation between χ(μ̲) and ν(μ̲) that belongs to the definitions
around (151), outside this package. On the second line of (277), see S02.

**R08 — scan 121 — claim without proof — KEEP, as routine.** (279) needs [Π,Π] to be normal in 𝓜. It is, because
[Π,Π] is characteristic in the normal subgroup Π. In the profinite case its closure is meant.

**R09 — scan 121 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.** The remark « on aurait pu prendre un quotient de Π_ab
invariant par action de Γ » is pursued on scan 130. There the Proposition starts from « un quotient M de Π, par un
sous-groupe distingué dans 𝓜, ou ce qui revient au m̄ un quotient de Π_ab stable par l'action de Γ » (see S04).

**R10 — scan 121 — own idea — KEEP.** Separating the hypotheses for the universal cover, the universal abelian cover
and pro-objects matches the margin note (« Contexte discret … ou profini … ») and the open question R31.

**R11 — scan 122 — claim without proof — KEEP.** Writing 𝒳′ as the twist of 𝒳′₀ by the M-torsor E (« ≃|= fibre de
𝒳′₀ en x₀ ») is standard, but the page does not construct it.

**R12 — scans 122–123 — cancelled text — KEEP.** The cancelled route gives a canonical action Θ₀ of
𝔖 = 𝓜/[Π,Π] on 𝒳′₀ = 𝒳̃/[Π,Π] and compares an arbitrary Θ with it. It was abandoned for the affine-group description
of scans 123–124, and 𝔖 was renamed M̃ in the live text.

**R13 — scan 122 — own idea — KEEP.** The proposed comparison between the cancelled direct action and the affine
description is reasonable. Scans 125 and 127 in effect carry it out (lifts ↔ splittings, the canonical class).

**R14 — scan 123 — claim without proof — CORRECT.** « qui d'ailleurs se factorise nécessairement par M̃ » has an
immediate reason on the page. Π acts on E through Π → M by translations, so [Π,Π] acts trivially, and the action of
𝓜 factors through 𝓜/[Π,Π] = M̃ (278). It is not an unproved claim.

**R15 — scan 123 — implication not pursued — KEEP, reading settled.** (280) now reads g(λ·x) = γ_M(λ)·gx; the
first pass also offered g_M(λ). The proof line follows: g(λ.x) = (gλg⁻¹)(gx). The formula says exactly that g acts
by an affine map with linear part γ_M, which is the step to (281).

**R16 — scan 124 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The equivalence between the 𝒳′ and the couples (E, Θ_M̃) is stated
without writing out the quasi-inverse functor. It is restated as part of the Proposition on scan 131.

**R17 — scan 124 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.** It is pursued at once on scan 125. A lift corresponds to an
isomorphism of the pulled-back extension with 𝔖 (= M̃). Existence holds if and only if c²(𝔖) ∈ H²(Γ,M) is zero (the
first (282)). The lifts form a torsor under Z¹(Γ,M), and their M-conjugacy classes correspond to H¹(Γ,M).

**R18 — scan 124 — own idea — KEEP.** Tracking how a change of E conjugates Θ_M̃ is what scan 129 does with
E → Scind (285) and the fibres u = y − x ∈ M^Γ.

**R19 — scan 125 — claim without proof (standard) — KEEP.** The H²/Z¹/H¹ statements are standard for an extension
with abelian kernel and a fixed action of Γ on M. The label (282) is used twice: here and for the diagram on scan
127.

**R20 — scan 125 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** The margin note now reads « les scindages de Aff(E) déterminés
par les pts de E — dépt de l'origine … », and it addresses the question raised here (see S05).

**R21 — scan 126 — claim without proof (reference) — KEEP.** The construction cited as « § 49 VI » (with bars over
and under VI) lies outside this package. The page takes from it 𝔖^{+∧}/μ_𝔖 ≃ 𝓜⁺₀,₃/[Π̂₀,₃,Π̂₀,₃] ⊂ M̃ and the
canonical homomorphism M̃ → G_{ρ,σ̃}.

**R22 — scan 126 — own idea — KEEP.** A notation table is useful. The margin NB already gives the dictionary: M
becomes M̂, 𝓜 becomes 𝓜₀,₃, π becomes Π̂₀,₃. The final display writes the commutator with round brackets,
𝓜₀,₃/(Π̂₀,₃,Π̂₀,₃).

**R23 — scan 127 — claim without proof — KEEP, completed by S06.** « d'où il résulte que l'ext. M̃ de Γ par M̂ l'est
aussi » follows by pulling back along (282). The second route, through « l'existence de la tour de Fermat », is only
announced.

**R24 — scan 127 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** « canonique (mais non unique !) » and the margin « H¹(Γ,M̂) est
un groupe fort gros ! — Mais voir …, cf Th. p. 716 » defer the study of the class to a theorem on ms. p. 716, beyond
this package.

**R25 — scan 127 — own idea — KEEP.** Computing the map on splittings induced by χ × id_𝔖₃ is the natural check. The
first letter of that label is written over another letter, and χ remains a reading in doubt.

**R26 — scan 128 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The coimage M̃̃ of Θ_M̃ is a quotient in which M maps injectively
(« tel que M ↪ M̃̃ soit injectif ») and Γ̃ = M̃̃/Im M acts faithfully (284). Both properties are stated without proof;
they follow from Θ_M̃ restricting to id_M and from the definition of Γ̃ as the image in Aut(M).

**R27 — scan 128 — implication not pursued — KEEP.** « n'est pas quelconque » announces the extra structure, a
class of M-conjugacy of splittings, which scan 129 then describes.

**R28 — scan 129 — claim without proof — KEEP.** Note the correction in the ink: Γ̃ « opère \deleted{injectivement}
\inserted{fidèlement} sur M ». The triviality of (283) and the class of splittings defined by E are stated without
proof; R35 and S05 give the mechanism.

**R29 — scan 129 — implication not pursued — CORRECT.** The first pass's remark that, for M^Γ ≠ 0, the orbit of
splittings is controlled by the quotient of E by M^Γ is stated by the author on scan 132: (291),
M^Γ\E ↪ Scind(M̃ ext. de Γ par M), a mono. It is pursued.

**R30 — scan 129 — own idea — KEEP.** A test with M^Γ ≠ 0 (two points of E differing by an invariant element give
the same splitting) checks the criterion (286) directly.

**R31 — scan 130 — question — KEEP.** « il faudrait expliciter pour bien faire les hypothèses raisonnables à faire
pour tenir compte de la topologie profinie de Γ… » is an explicit open task (continuity of the action, the class of
profinite coverings, closed commutator subgroups).

**R32 — scan 130 — own idea — KEEP, sharpened by S04.** The test with a non-abelian invariant quotient is apt. The
« ce qui revient au m̄ » holds only for abelian M, and the text adds « où M est comm. » two lines below.

**R33 — scan 131 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The equivalence between the 𝒳′ (with isomorphisms) and the couples
(E, Θ_M̃), with Θ_M̃ a homomorphism of extensions (289), is stated without proof. In the profinite case it depends on
the hypotheses left open in R31.

**R34 — scan 132 — claim without proof — KEEP, reading corrected.** The ink reads « correspondent aux "translations"
par des éléments de M^Γ (= Cent(M̃)) », not « par un élément ». The identification with translations is stated, and
so is the inserted M^Γ = Cent(M̃); see S03 for the latter.

**R35 — scan 132 — claim without proof — KEEP, reading corrected.** The author first wrote « L'image (290) … » and
struck it for « L'hom (290) se|de factorise par M^Γ\E en un mono ». The reconstruction of E when M^Γ = 0 follows
from (291) together with the description of the image as the given class of conjugacy. Equivariance is asserted on
scan 133.

**R36 — scan 132 — own idea — KEEP.** Comparing torsors with the same M^Γ\E shows what (291) loses when M^Γ ≠ 0.

**R37 — scan 133 — claim without proof — KEEP.** « ne fait pas sortir d'une classe de M-conjugaison, m̄ en conjuguant
par des éléments quelconques de M̃ » is immediate once a splitting s is fixed: every g ∈ M̃ is m·s(γ), and
conjugation by s(γ) carries the class of s to itself. The page does not write this out.

**R38 — scan 133 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The page itself reads (292) as « deux scindages d'une ext. triviale
de Γ̃ par M soient conjugués par un élément unique de M ». H¹ = 0 gives the conjugacy and H⁰ = M^Γ = 0 the
uniqueness. Rigidity then follows « à cause de M^Γ = {0} », as inserted.

**R39 — scan 133 — own idea — KEEP, carried out.** For Γ̃ = ℤ/2 acting by −1 on M = ℤ/n with n odd:
M^Γ = 0, and H¹ = ker(1+σ)/(1−σ)M = M/2M = 0. So (292) holds and the Corollary applies. More generally (S08),
(292) holds whenever M is finite of order prime to |Γ̃| and M^Γ = 0.

**R40 — scan 133 — claim without proof — KEEP.** The sentence « correspondent biunivoquement aux groupes quotients
M̃̃ » breaks off at the end of the package; its conditions are on the next scan.

## Notes added in the second pass (own)

**S01 — scan 116 — own idea (verification), high.** The isomorphism M ≃ K of (254) holds over ℤ. In (253), K is
{(α₀,α₁,β) : 2α₁ − α₀ − 3β = 0} ≅ ℤ² via (α₁,β). The map (255), λ = c₀λ₀ + c₁λ₁ ↦ (α₁,β) = (2c₁ − c₀, c₁ − c₀),
has determinant (−1)(1) − (2)(−1) = 1. So the uniqueness « conjugués par une unique section de M̲ » holds over every
base S, including residue characteristics 2 and 3.

**S02 — scans 119–120 — implication not pursued (own), medium.** (276) states u′_μ̲(σ′) = σ′ and (274) gives σ′ = σ,
while (277) states u′_μ̲(σ) = ω^{ν₂}σ. The two agree only because u_{ρ′,σ′} takes values in G̲_{ρ,σ}/μ_G for
μ̲ ∈ 𝕆₁₂*, and in G̲_{ρ,σ} only for μ̲ ∈ 𝕆₁₂*'' (275), where ν₂ = 0. So (276) is to be read modulo μ_G, as the
parenthesis « (= σ dans G̲_{ρ,σ}/μ_G …) » of (277) says. The factor ω^{ν₂} is the obstruction of R04 seen on a single
element.

**S03 — scan 132 — claim without proof (own), medium.** The inserted « M^Γ = Cent(M̃) » holds for M ∩ Cent(M̃), the
elements of M central in M̃. It equals the whole centre only if no element outside M is central. An element g over γ
that is central centralises M, so γ acts trivially on M. This holds in the faithful quotient M̃̃ of (283)–(284), but
for M̃ itself it needs Γ → Aut(M) injective, which the general setting does not assume.

**S04 — scan 130 — claim without proof (own), high.** In « un quotient M de Π, par un sous-groupe distingué dans 𝓜,
ou ce qui revient au m̄ un quotient de Π_ab stable par l'action de Γ », the equivalence presupposes that M is
commutative. A quotient of Π by a subgroup normal in 𝓜 factors through Π_ab only if it is abelian. The text adds
« où M est comm. » after (287), so the hypothesis is intended but stated after its use.

**S05 — scan 125 — claim without proof (own, from the author's margin), medium.** The margin « les scindages de
Aff(E) déterminés par les pts de E — dépt de l'origine … » states the standard fact behind « Aff(E) scindé ». A point
x ∈ E gives the splitting Aut(M) → Aff(E), φ ↦ (the affine map fixing x with linear part φ). Replacing x by x + u
conjugates this splitting by the translation u. This is the map E → Scind of (285)/(290), and its fibres are the
M^Γ-orbits of (286)/(291). The margin reading is partly uncertain (« dépt », « intérieurs »).

**S06 — scan 127 — implication not pursued (own), high.** In (282) the left vertical arrow is the identity of M̂, so
M̃ is the pull-back of G_{ρ,σ̃} along χ × id_𝔖₃: Γ → Ẑ*×𝔖₃ (the canonical map to the fibre product is a morphism
of extensions of Γ by M̂, hence an isomorphism). Every splitting of G_{ρ,σ̃} therefore pulls back to a splitting of
M̃ over Γ. A unique M̂-conjugacy class downstairs gives a canonical class upstairs, which need not be the only one,
since H¹(Γ,M̂) is large (margin).

**S07 — scans 118 and 132 — own idea (two author's slips, kept as written).** In the profinite Corollary of scan 118
the bijection is stated for « scindages de G″_{ρ,σ} sur Ẑ*×H⁺ », but G″_{ρ,σ} is an extension of Ẑ*″×H⁺, as the
previous sentence says. On scan 132, « l'extension (287) de Γ par M̃ » should be « M̃ de Γ par M »: (287) is the
extension M̃ of Γ by M. Both stay as written in the transcription.

**S08 — scan 133 — implication not pursued (own), high.** If M is finite of order prime to |Γ̃|, then
H¹(Γ̃,M) = 0 automatically, and (292) reduces to M^Γ = 0. The rigidity of the Corollary therefore covers all
coprime cases without invariants. The profinite case of interest (M̂, with Γ̃ the image of Γ_ℚ × 𝔖₃) is not
coprime. The margin of scan 127 (« H¹(Γ,M̂) est un groupe fort gros ! ») concerns Γ itself; by inflation–restriction,
H¹(Γ̃,M̂) is only the part of it inflated from Γ̃. These pages do not settle whether (292) holds for Γ̃ there.


# ===== LM_C4-134-152 (first-pass notes) =====

# LM_C4-134-152 — Notes to the diplomatic transcription

## Source, scope, and reading conventions

This package transcribes all 19 supplied scan files, 134–152, from Alexander Grothendieck's *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier archive cote 140-4. The supplied images are the sole transcription source; no published transcription or text from outside this package was used to complete a reading. The source reference appears at the end of this file.

Scan numbers follow the supplied filenames. They differ from the small handwritten and printed archive numerals, which run from 133 to 151. The author's visible top-of-page numbers are 715–723 on alternate scans, 135–151; only these are encoded by `\mspage`. The lower-left handwritten archive numerals are retained as marginal matter. Every printed institutional footer is copied, word for word, into non-rendering LaTeX comments immediately after its scan's manuscript text, so that the archive apparatus remains distinct from the manuscript.

The transcription is a fragment, without a preamble or macro definitions. It uses the requested editorial macros with ordinary LaTeX mathematics, TikZ, and tikz-cd; an enclosing document must define the editorial macros and supply the usual mathematics and graphics packages. A temporary wrapper with those definitions was used to syntax-check all 19 scans. This check does not settle a doubtful reading or guarantee the layout of another editor's wrapper.

The handwritten large double-curved group letter is rendered `\mathfrak G`, distinct from ordinary `G_{\rho,\sigma}`; the permutation-group letter is rendered `\mathfrak S_3`. The larger arithmetic group letter is represented by `\mathcal M`, and the module by `M`. The long Jacobian glyph is represented by `\mathcal J`. These typographic identifications follow the visible forms; ambiguous particular occurrences and overwritten accents remain marked. The tilde in `G_{\rho,\widetilde\sigma}` belongs to the subscript. The barred wavy m is consistently rendered `\overline{\mathrm m}`, without expanding it in the transcription.

## 1. Table of doubts

Line positions are approximate manuscript positions, not lines in the LaTeX file. Formula numbers, neighboring words, and marginal positions provide additional locators. Confidence describes the visible ink, not the plausibility of a mathematical argument. Alternatives remain candidates for checking; none is a silent correction. A single row may cover repeated instances of the same uncertain glyph or overwritten subscript on one scan.
