## 3. Legibility and limits

The main prose and most active formulas are readable, but many word endings are reduced to short strokes. The least secure material is the abandoned quotient notation on scan 134; the cancellation and small inserted formulas on 136; central-kernel annotations on 139–140; overwritten base letters on 143 and 146; the two diagonal notes on 147; retouched cohomology symbols on 148; and the cancellations and border-adjacent writing on 149–152. The lower diagonal note on 147 remains particularly uncertain even after rotated enlargements and an independent reread. On scan 152 the last word reaches the black border: only the visible stem “axiomatiq” is retained, without supplying an unseen ending.

Each scan was read whole, transcribed, reread against the image, and inventoried. An independent comparison additionally checked scans 134–138 and the dense marginal notes on 147; selected subsequent pages were checked again during assembly. The process improved several small readings, but it cannot make the remaining overwritten ink certain. Tentative readings are retained in place, including canceled mathematical material; the doubt marks should be consulted before quoting those passages as definitive.

Apparent surprises have been preserved: the E-shaped letter in “E-torseur,” repeated “classes,” the correction of “trois” to “deux,” the asymmetrical cohomology degrees in the lower note on 147, repeated or overwritten numbering, and open or unfinished expressions. Plain connecting strokes in diagrams remain headless where no arrowhead is securely visible. Marginal emphasis strokes are reproduced in TikZ. Paragraph continuity across scan boundaries is retained, including the broken word between 137 and 138. The inventory includes the main text, subordinate displays, diagrams, marginal matter, insertions, deletions, emphasis drawings, and printed footer apparatus.

## 4. Research notes — English

These notes are separate from the diplomatic transcription and never determine its readings. Every scan locator below is a pinpoint reference to Grothendieck (1981), as identified in Section 5. “Unproved claim” means that a proof is not supplied at the cited location; it does not mean that the claim remains unproved elsewhere in the manuscript or in the literature. Questions and expectations are grouped under “conjecture.” Suggestions labelled “own idea” are exploratory, with confidence and a reason; they are not presented as established results or claims of novelty. Quoted words with paleographic uncertainty must be read with the corresponding table entry.

### Scans 134–138

- **Scan 134, conditions a)–c) and paragraph beginning “Pour un tel quotient”; unpursued implication.** The manuscript reconstructs E as “l'ensemble de tous les scindages” and relates conjugation to its torsor structure. A separate verification could formulate precisely the equivalence between quotients satisfying these three conditions and the relevant affine actions; the visible “E-torseur” has not been corrected to another group name in the transcription.
- **Scan 134, final paragraph; unproved claim / backward reference.** “Nous avons vérifié les trois relations (292)” refers to evidence outside this package. Any mathematical audit must recover those hypotheses and the earlier construction rather than treat the present backward reference as a proof contained in these scans.


- **Scan 135, “un objet de la catégorie”; unproved claim / backward reference.** The preceding corollary is invoked to construct an object “à isom unique près” from the surjective homomorphism. A proof audit should check the exact notion of morphism in that earlier category, since existence of a cover and uniqueness of an isomorphism are different assertions.
- **Scan 135, last paragraph; unproved claim, continued on scan 136.** The tower of Fermat covers is said to supply “très exactement un tel objet”. Verifying this requires matching the group action and geometric abelianization with the conditions just stated; this is a question about the construction, not a suggestion to replace the manuscript's formulas by a known version.


- **Scan 136, first paragraph; conjecture / authorial expectation.** “Je n'aurais certes pas suspecté” that the indicated subextension “soit scindée” records how the Fermat tower prompted the splitting claim. Recovering the exact subextension and its central quotient from preceding pages would clarify the geometric input into this surprise.
- **Scan 136, double-marked paragraph; conjecture.** “La conjecture qui se présente tout de suite” is that the two proétale covers are isomorphic, with a unique isomorphism. It should be tracked even though the following pages return to its resolution; the uniqueness assertion has its own cohomological content.
- **Scan 136, (294)–(295) and diagonal note; unpursued implication.** The displayed exact sequence and invariant-vanishing claim reduce the displayed H¹ to Galois invariants in H¹(S₃,hat M). A proof reconstruction should specify the coefficient topology and the meaning of continuous cohomology rather than silently import a discrete-coefficient version.
- **Scan 136, final equality; unproved claim.** The equality identifying H¹(S₃,hat M) with H¹(S₃,M) tensor profinite Z is asserted here without a separate argument. Establishing it would require a justified passage between the integral module, its completion, and degree-one cohomology.


- **Scan 137, opening question; conjecture / question.** The author asks to calculate “H¹(S₃,M) ?” and then invokes the preceding study of splittings. This local question is answered on the same page; its logical dependence on the earlier splitting theorem should remain explicit.
- **Scan 137, (296)–(297); unproved claim / backward reference.** The integral vanishing is extracted from the assertion that two splittings are conjugate by a unique element. A reconstruction should separately identify how H⁰ governs the stabilizer of a splitting and H¹ governs conjugacy classes, then justify the completion step used on the second line.
- **Scan 137, theorem; unpursued implication.** The theorem specifies geometric universal abelianization together with an arithmetic and permutation-group descent structure, and concludes uniqueness of the isomorphism after continuation onto scan 138. The restriction to that descent structure is essential to check; the bare geometric cover has a different automorphism problem.
- **Scan 137, theorem; own idea.** Compare the groupoid of descent data with the groupoid of splittings of the displayed extension, keeping automorphism groups rather than only isomorphism classes. **Confidence: medium**, because the manuscript explicitly couples unique conjugacy with vanishing in degrees zero and one, but the precise equivalence needs the omitted surrounding definitions.


- **Scan 138, first paragraph after theorem; unproved claim / backward reference.** The author says existence was obtained “de deux façons”, through the Fermat tower and the general theory of §49 III. Comparing the two constructions requires recovering that earlier theory and the hypotheses satisfied by each object; uniqueness here supplies the proposed identification.
- **Scan 138, last paragraph; unpursued implication.** The author emphasizes that the vanishing follows “sans rien connaître sur ΓQ”. This points to a formal sufficient criterion: vanishing in degrees zero and one for the permutation action can eliminate the corresponding extension-class ambiguity before any arithmetic cohomology is calculated.
- **Scan 138, display beneath “gros”; own idea.** Check the meaning of the completion symbol over Q* against the earlier definition of T∞, and distinguish full profinite completion from a completion defined by power subgroups. **Confidence: medium**, because the manuscript writes a completion-like identification under the brace, while the precise topology is not specified on this page; this is a verification task, not a claim that the displayed identification is false.

### Scans 139–143

These notes concern only what these five scans state or leave to investigate. A statement called “unproved” below is not claimed to be unproved in the whole manuscript: the proof may be in preceding or subsequent pages. Suggestions labelled **own idea** are exploratory and are neither established results nor claims of novelty.

#### 139-R1

- **Scan / location:** 139, opening paragraph and first three displays.
- **Kind:** Unproved claim.
- **Textual anchor:** “élucide complètement … la signification arithmético-géométrique”.
- **Note:** Grothendieck asserts that the preceding theorem supplies the arithmetic and geometric meaning of the algebraically obtained homomorphism, or at least of its quotient by the central kernel. A complete verification should identify the algebraic map with the action on the geometric object, retain the distinction between the original and quotient targets, and state which parts depend on the preceding theorem rather than on the present argument.

#### 139-R2

- **Scan / location:** 139, lower paragraph.
- **Kind:** Unpursued implication.
- **Textual anchor:** “opérant fidèlement” and “opérait trivialement”.
- **Note:** The passage to the quotient group is motivated by the need for a faithful action on the completed module, whereas the center of the earlier acting group acts trivially. This raises the precise question of what geometric information the faithful quotient retains and what additional data is required to detect the central kernel. The page makes the contrast explicit but does not construct that extra data here.

#### 140-R1

- **Scan / location:** 140, paragraph preceding (298).
- **Kind:** Unproved claim.
- **Textual anchor:** “ce revêtement n'est autre que le prorevêtement de Fermat”.
- **Note:** The action on the set of splittings is identified with the Fermat procover. To make this identification explicit, one would need a compatible description at finite levels, its transition maps, and the equivariance of the resulting inverse system. The handwritten insertion identifying the two fundamental groups is part of the asserted interpretation and should be checked together with the precise base and quotient conventions.

#### 140-R2

- **Scan / location:** 140, diagram (298), continuing at the top of 141.
- **Kind:** Unproved claim.
- **Textual anchor:** “comme image inverse de l'extension”.
- **Note:** Diagram (298) is used to interpret the arithmetic group as a pullback central extension. A proof should verify the cartesian property, the identification of the two displayed kernels, and compatibility with the quotient homomorphism. If the groups are being considered topologically, the category and continuity requirements should be made explicit as well.

#### 141-R1

- **Scan / location:** 141, last paragraph, continuing in the first paragraph of 142.
- **Kind:** Unpursued implication.
- **Textual anchor:** “calculs en apparence assez différents” and “canoniquement isomorphes”.
- **Note:** Two constructions of central extensions are said to produce a canonical isomorphism despite their rather different computations. It would be useful to isolate exactly which identifications make the isomorphism canonical, and to determine whether a comparison of the induced quotient maps alone suffices or whether a specified identification of the central kernels is essential. The sentence crosses the scan boundary and should be read as one claim.

#### 142-R1

- **Scan / location:** 142, middle paragraph.
- **Kind:** Conjecture / expectation.
- **Textual anchor:** “une explication générale de ce fait” and “aucune difficulté”.
- **Note:** Grothendieck expects a general explanation of the canonical agreement of the two central extensions. His suggested reason is that the starting quotient came from the larger covering group rather than from its quotient by the central involution. A rigorous formulation would need a comparison principle for these two lift constructions and a proof of its applicability to the maps under discussion; the opening verb and the exact covering noun remain paleographically uncertain.

#### 142-R2

- **Scan / location:** 142, last paragraph.
- **Kind:** Conjecture / desired construction.
- **Textual anchor:** “il faudrait interpréter” and “une ‘fibre type’ ‘pas trop grosse’”.
- **Note:** The page asks for a geometric interpretation of the original target as the automorphism group of a reasonably small fibre type on which it acts nontrivially. The problem is more specific than finding any representation: the object should explain the original homomorphism in the manner already supplied by the Fermat tower for its quotient. The manuscript explicitly says that inventing such an object is the difficulty.

#### 142-R3

- **Scan / location:** 142, last paragraph, in relation to (298) on 140.
- **Kind:** Own idea.
- **Confidence:** Low — the central extension suggests the form of an enhancement, but the pages do not supply the necessary descent or existence data.
- **Note:** One could test whether an enhancement of the splitting object by a compatible torsor under the displayed central kernel makes that kernel act nontrivially. Such an enhancement would have to be natural under the relevant base changes and recover the known quotient action after forgetting it. This is only a candidate direction for the requested fibre type, not a construction established by these scans.

#### 143-R1

- **Scan / location:** 143, formulas (299)–(300) and final paragraph.
- **Kind:** Unproved claim.
- **Textual anchor:** “dépend … de … pour iso et images inverses” and “une section cartésienne”.
- **Note:** The construction from the degree-three étale cover, the quotient of its integral permutation sheaf, and the Tate-type factor is asserted to be functorial for isomorphisms and pullbacks. A proof should identify the category of families, the pushforward and diagonal maps, and the base-change isomorphisms compatible with the quotient and tensor product. The exact base letter in (299) is overwritten, so any mathematical exposition should first resolve that transcription issue rather than silently repairing it.

#### 143-R2

- **Scan / location:** 143, formulas (299)–(300).
- **Kind:** Own idea.
- **Confidence:** Medium as a proposed local calculation — the quotient formula and rank correction visibly suggest it, but no global conclusion is asserted here.
- **Note:** After choosing a local ordering of the three sheets, one can examine the displayed quotient through the elementary model of a rank-three permutation lattice modulo its diagonal. Tracking the permutations and then the completed twist could provide an explicit check of the resulting rank-two local system and its descent. This calculation should remain separate from the diplomatic text and should not be used to replace the manuscript's canceled “trois” by its inserted “deux”.

### Scans 144–148

These are source-based reading notes, not a literature survey. Statements about the manuscript refer to the attached scans of Grothendieck (1981); speculative extensions are labelled **own idea**, with confidence and reason, and do not modify the diplomatic text.

1. **Scan 144, lines 1–12 — unpursued implication.** The Fermat tower is reformulated as the assignment of a torsor “compatible aux iso. et chgts de base,” with an additional condition that the induced homomorphism be “l'hom canonique.” A detailed reconstruction should distinguish the uniqueness of such a compatible assignment from uniqueness for an individual base and should keep track of the canonical homomorphism condition rather than dropping it.

2. **Scan 145, final paragraph — conjecture / question.** “Y-a-t-il une description remarquable du torseur de Fermat” asks for an interpretation in terms of the relative elliptic curve. The preceding exact sequence (303) suggests that the required description must include both a torsor for the Tate module of the generalized Jacobian and a section of its quotient torsor; the question is retained as open here even if later manuscript pages supply a construction.

3. **Scan 145, (302)–(304) — unproved claim.** “On en déduit une suite exacte” passes from the generalized-Jacobian extension to a sequence of full Tate modules. A proof would need to justify exactness through the inverse system, specify the transition maps in the notation `{}_nG`, and retain the connected-fibre qualification inserted into the description of the smooth commutative group scheme.

4. **Scan 145, following (304) — own idea.** One could make the torsor-and-section description a concrete descent construction, using local liftings of the quotient-torsor section to recover an `M_1`-torsor. **Confidence: high as a proof strategy**, because the displayed short exact sequence is exactly the datum such a local-lifting argument uses; no claim of novelty or of a complete proof is intended.

5. **Scan 146, (305)–(306) and final paragraph — unpursued implication.** The canonical map to the degree-one component is required to “commute aux opérations” of the permutation group. The relevant torsor therefore carries an equivariant structure, not merely its ordinary torsor structure; subsequent uniqueness assertions should be interpreted in that equivariant setting. The original shifts in base notation on this page should be resolved before using it for a statement over all schemes.

6. **Scan 147, lines 1–7 and (307) — conjecture / expectation.** “Or je présume qu'on a” introduces the vanishing of equivariant cohomology in degrees zero and one. The page begins an argument for the first vanishing and announces a spectral sequence for the second; the expectation should be tracked independently of the completion of its proof on the following page.

7. **Scan 147, upper diagonal note beside (308) — unpursued implication.** The warning says that the displayed cyclic-ratio isomorphism “commute à” the alternating subgroup, but not to the full permutation group. Any use of the quotient-torus/kernel identification in the equivariant calculation therefore needs a precise comparison of the two actions; the warning must not disappear when the formula is rewritten.

8. **Scan 147, lower diagonal note — own idea.** The equivariance warning suggests checking the action of a transposition explicitly on the cyclic-ratio map, before using the kernel model to identify fixed points. **Confidence: high as a diagnostic step**, since the note distinguishes the index-two subgroup from the full permutation group; it does not by itself establish the corrected cohomology computation, and the note's compressed prose remains uncertain.

9. **Scan 148, (309) — unproved claim / reference to earlier work.** “Les calculs faits par ailleurs montrent” asserts vanishing in degrees zero and one for the module obtained by tensoring with an arbitrary integer module. A verification needs the precise permutation-group action on the lattice and must preserve the preceding page's warning that one convenient identification is only equivariant for the alternating subgroup.

10. **Scan 148, left margin and conclusion (310) — unpursued implication.** “On n'a pas besoin de la relation” `Pic(S)=0` explicitly removes a hypothesis used in the preliminary calculation, and the conclusion is stated “pour” every scheme S. To substantiate this generality, one should follow both low-degree terms of the spectral sequence and apply (309) to the two different coefficient groups mentioned in the parenthesis, including the inserted Picard-group example.

11. **Scan 148, (309) — own idea.** A useful independent check would start with the diagonal inclusion into the three-coordinate permutation module, tensor with a completely arbitrary abelian group, and compare the resulting low-degree cohomology sequences. **Confidence: high as a verification strategy**, because it uses the quotient presentation already written in (308) and avoids treating the cyclic-ratio presentation as fully equivariant; no proof or novelty claim is being asserted here.

### Scans 149–152

These notes are source-based questions and possible lines of investigation, not verified claims about the subsequent literature. Quoted readings with paleographic uncertainty should be checked against the corresponding doubt entry.

1. **Scan 149, proposition, lines 1–6 — unproved claim.** The proposition asserts “une unique trivialisation” for every `(S_3,J^0)`-torsor over an arbitrary base. A proof audit should identify precisely the topology of the torsor and how the preceding degree-zero and degree-one vanishing statements give both existence and uniqueness uniformly after base change.
2. **Scan 149, Corollaire 1, (311) — unproved claim.** “Un unique morphisme” is asserted with compatibility under the three-point symmetry group and with the identity induced on `J^0`. Its derivation should spell out the generalized Jacobian's universal property and resolve the source correction below the displayed homomorphism without silently replacing the manuscript's notation.
3. **Scan 149, diagonal NB, (312) — unpursued implication.** The note places `U_1` in the three-coordinate torus modulo the diagonal, with equation `x_0+x_1+x_∞=0`. This gives a concrete model in which to test equivariance and the relation between the distinguished three boundary points and the map in (311).
4. **Scan 150, Corollaire 3 continued, (313)–(314) — unproved claim.** For a Brauer–Severi curve with an étale divisor of degree three, the text asserts “un unique morphisme” to the degree-zero generalized Jacobian, equivariant for the displayed automorphism group and inducing the identity on that Jacobian. The descent from a split three-point projective line should be checked, including compatibility of the morphism with changes of the local ordering of the divisor.
5. **Scan 150, description of `M_0(S'/S)` — unpursued implication.** The coefficient object is described as obtained “en le tordant” through the three-point symmetry group's action. The relation between this explicit twisted lattice and the torus appearing in (313) is worth recording functorially, with all pullback identifications made explicit.
6. **Scan 150, (313), own idea — confidence: medium.** Test the construction on bases where the three-point étale divisor is not split, using the quotient-of-permutation-lattice description to calculate transition maps. The reason for this suggestion is that the manuscript states the twisting construction but does not compute a nonsplit example on these pages; no novelty or general conclusion is claimed.
7. **Scan 151, (315)–(317) — unproved claim.** “Il suffit de prendre” introduces the coordinate formula `ψ(z)=(-z,z−1,1)` and two alternative representatives. A complete verification should check invertibility on the open curve, the three listed boundary values, and equivariance under the three-point permutations; these checks would also anchor the descent used on scan 150.
8. **Scan 151, final paragraph — unpursued implication.** The elliptic-curve datum “modulo symétrie” is related to a morphism into `M'_{1,1}`. The precise meaning of this moduli object should be recovered from the surrounding manuscript before drawing conclusions about the information retained or forgotten by that morphism.
9. **Scan 151, (317), own idea — confidence: high as a verification task only.** Work out the two basic transformations `z↦1−z` and `z↦1/z` directly on the displayed three-coordinate representative and compare the result with coordinate permutation modulo a common scalar. The formula is explicit enough to make this a useful elementary check, but no claim of a new construction is intended.
10. **Scan 152, lines 1–5 — unproved claim.** Under the hypothesis “2 inv. sur S,” the datum of `X` modulo its involution is said to correspond to `(Y,S')` together with “une section de U.” A proof should formulate the objects and their isomorphisms precisely and check both directions of this correspondence, including the dependence on the extra section.
11. **Scan 152, middle paragraph — unproved claim.** The author says that `Y` “se récupère” from the three-point étale datum, then writes the twisted projective line and the corresponding open subscheme. One should verify the descent maps and their compatibility with the subsequent canonical embedding into the generalized Jacobian.
12. **Scan 152, (318), final line — conjecture / expectation.** The canonical morphism is followed by “qui doit être aisé à caractériser” and the clipped stem “axiomatiq.” This is an explicit expectation of an axiomatic characterization; a proof would first have to state the proposed axioms and establish the claimed uniqueness or canonicity under them.
13. **Scan 152, (318), own idea — confidence: medium.** Try to characterize the displayed map by its factorization through the open three-point curve and the equivariant universal property stated on scans 149–150. The suggestion is prompted by the construction immediately preceding (318); the pages do not establish that these conditions are sufficient, so it remains a test to undertake rather than a conclusion.

## 5. Source reference

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, cote 140-4, supplied scan files 134–152; archive file described as pages 650bis–787]. Université de Montpellier.

The institution's printed source address on every supplied image is [grothendieck.umontpellier.fr](https://grothendieck.umontpellier.fr). The reference identifies the supplied primary source; no external edition or literature search was used for these transcription and research notes.

This edition transcribes the 19 supplied image files numbered **153–171** from Alexander Grothendieck’s *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* (1981), Montpellier archive cote 140-4, archive-file range 650bis–787. All readings and quotations derive from those supplied images. No published transcription, OCR transcript, or external mathematical source was used to fill gaps or decide what the mathematics should say.

The filename scan number controls `\scanpage{N}`. The lower-left pencilled number, retained with `\margin{…}`, is consistently one lower. Only a visible authorial running number receives `\mspage{…}`; no missing authorial number is inferred. The printed institutional address, URL, and repeated footer number are archival apparatus and are not included in the handwritten transcription.

## 4. Legibility and consequential reading decisions

The main prose is usually readable, but the hand is highly compressed and frequent cancellations obscure whole endings, indices, and equation numbers. Whole-page readings were checked against enlarged overlapping regions, with additional crops for dense revisions. Several pages received an independent second reading; this reduces, but cannot eliminate, transcription error. The least secure portions are the overwritten formula numbers on scan 153, the compressed `tri-` word and cancelled indices on scan 155, the group superscripts on scans 156–158, the rewritten coordinate conditions and definition on scan 159, the crowded automorphism condition on scan 162, and the superscript annotations on scan 166. The inserted dimension condition on scan 167 and the revised final paragraph on scan 171 also remain weak points.

The three `\illegible{1}` occurrences concern physically obscured words: the opening cancellation on scan 153, the cancelled word in item 1 on scan 155, and the cancelled annotation on scan 166. Mathematical symbols, including heavily crossed ones, instead carry proposed readings inside `\unsure{…}`. In particular, the last group-superscript shape is left as opposing arrows with a sharp-like alternative where the distinction remains unresolved, and the N alphabet in the later segment is explicitly doubtful.

Several tempting normalizations have deliberately not been made. The apparent missing exponent on the first coordinate in the second line of (332), uncertain signs in that display, repeated or inconsistent equation references, the duplicated-looking authorial page number on scan 157, and the awkward surviving wording on scans 163 and 166 remain visible. Both twists in (357) and the following question on scan 167 read `(-1)` on enlargement; the twists in (358) and (365) also visibly include a minus, while the two less clear occurrences on scan 168 retain explicit alternatives. The full `Π₁` written in the scan-167 question is not changed to its cyclotomic kernel.

On scan 169, the insertion « la tour des » attaches to « images inverses », rather than forming a separate object beside (360); the repeated label (339) is retained. On scan 171, the word before the ramification insertion is « ramification », the three coordinate tuples retain their small superscript dots, and the insertion « de degré n en » is attached to the revised phrase. Ordinary Fermat-curve U and the more ornate universal-curve letter remain distinct. The all-plus equation is transcribed literally, and the excerpt stops before any completed genus formula.

## 5. Research notes — separate from the transcription

The following items distinguish the author’s questions and expectations, claims requiring verification, consequences not pursued on the cited page, and the transcriber’s own suggested checks. They are source-bound research prompts, not claims of originality or a survey of subsequent results. A point may be addressed later in the manuscript; its appearance here is still recorded. Quotations preserve brief readable wording, with ellipses used only in these notes. Inferences and proposed checks never determine an uncertain transcription reading. All manuscript references below use filename scan numbers (Grothendieck, 1981).

### R01

**Scan 153, last display — unproved claim.** “Ceci posé, on aura” introduces the identification of `F_∞(X̃/S)` with the fibre `p^{-1}(e)` of the universal isogeny. A verification should identify the finite-level torsors, transition maps, and action of the inverse-limit torsion group, rather than establish only a bijection of geometric fibres. The reference to (301), p. 719′ points outside this assigned page group (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 153).

### R02

**Scan 154, (324) and following — unproved claim.** The kernel of the canonical map is said to be the subgroup of “éléments ‘infiniment divisibles’.” A proof should specify whether this means a compatible system of division points or divisibility separately by every positive integer, and justify the relevant inverse-limit/cohomology passage in the topology being used (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 154).

### R03

**Scan 154, middle — unproved claim.** “On sait (et c'est … ‘élémentaire’) que ce noyau est réduit à zéro” is asserted for the reduced finite-type situation described in the paragraph. A proof needs to use the precise group represented by `J^0`, account for the descent from a splitting cover, and establish the required absence of infinitely divisible global sections; the cancelled words are not used to strengthen the claim (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 154).

### R04

**Scan 154, insertion `réduit` — own idea.** Test the need for the reducedness hypothesis on `Spec(Q[ε]/(ε²))` with the multiplicative group: each unit `1+aε` has the compatible divisions `1+(a/n)ε`, with the usual divisibility transition maps. This supplies a concrete model for how nilpotents can introduce nontrivial indefinitely divisible units; it is a proposed comparison with the manuscript's setting, not a claim about every group occurring there. **Confidence: high for the calculation, medium for its relevance**, because `(1+bε)^n=1+nbε` verifies the calculation directly, while the exact torus/descent identification still has to be checked (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 154).

### R05

**Scans 154–155, final sentence and numbered items — unpursued implication.** “La section e … est alors entièrement déterminée” is completed by the two data listed on scan 155: the indicated étale cover and the infinite torsor. One can ask for a precise reconstruction statement describing which pairs of such data occur, since uniqueness of the original section does not by itself furnish an existence criterion (Grothendieck, 1981, scans 154–155).

### R06

**Scan 156, bottom — unproved claim.** “Le premier hom. … a été caractérisé” refers to the earlier geometric description and its reduction modulo `μ_G`, followed by the citation of section 13 and the Fermat tower. The claim's proof is therefore external to this page group; a verification should trace the cited quotient and the action on the tower without assuming that the visibly uncertain formula number is correct (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 156).

### R07

**Scan 157, upper paragraph — unpursued implication.** The statement that the Fermat tower has been “décri[te]” through the homomorphism constructed “à la force du poignet” proposes a reversal of viewpoint between geometric towers and profinite group data. It would be useful to formulate exactly which equivalence relation on towers the homomorphism determines and which base-point choices remain implicit (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 157).

### R08

**Scan 157, middle — conjecture / expectation.** “Il resterait maintenant à trouver une explicitation arithmético-géométrique” explicitly announces unfinished work on the homomorphism from `Γ_Q` to the displayed extension of `Ẑ*` by `Ẑ`. The subsequent embedding and composite show a proposed route through the Fermat action, but the announced explanation is not completed within this page (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 157).

### R09

**Scan 157, lower diagrams — unproved claim.** The assertion “l'hom … apparaît comme le composé” identifies the arithmetic homomorphism with the map obtained through the indicated `κ_{1/2}` and Fermat action. A proof must compare choices of sections/base points and establish the commutativity of the diagram announced at the page end, which continues on the following scan (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 157).

### R10

**Scan 157, `κ_{1/2}` — own idea.** Use the explicitly written base-point label `1/2` to compare the resulting cocycle with finite Kummer fibres at that point, retaining the cyclotomic action throughout. **Confidence: medium**, because the displayed factorization suggests this test, but the detailed coordinate choice and normalization are not supplied on this page; no originality or established identification is claimed (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 157).

### R11

**Scan 158, (328)–(330).** **Kind: unproved claim.** “Cela signifie donc que l'on trouve l'hom. induit par (326)” identifies the arithmetic homomorphism through the larger affine representation attached to the Fermat tower. To check this assertion from first principles would require the constructions behind (325)–(327), the choice of fibre, and the equivariance of the displayed identifications; these pages do not reproduce that verification (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 158, eqs. 328–330).

### R12

**Scan 158, final paragraph.** **Kind: unproved claim.** The insertion says that the “pt 1/2” corresponds, “par l'iso. ψ (317),” to the written homogeneous triple. A proof needs the earlier map ψ and its coordinate convention; the present transcription must not use this claim to settle the overwritten sign or coordinate of the triple (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 158, final paragraph).

### R13

**Scan 159, (332)–(334).** **Kind: unproved claim.** “La fibre … s'identifie ainsi aux systèmes de solutions” and “Passant à la limite on trouve un iso.” assert finite-level coordinate descriptions and their compatible inverse-limit identification with a product of Kummer torsors. Verification would have to track the diagonal multiplicative-group quotient, the normalization of the last homogeneous coordinate, and the transition maps; the visibly inconsistent or uncertain signs are an editorial issue to resolve from the ink, not from that verification (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 159, eqs. 332–334).

### R14

**Scan 159, (333)–(335).** **Kind: unpursued implication.** The definition “T∞(ζ)=lim← T_n(ζ)” packages compatible roots into a torsor under T∞(G_m). This prompts a distinction between the finite-level torsors and the topology or site in which the inverse-limit torsor is being used, especially when comparing it with a geometric fibre of an infinite covering tower; that distinction is not developed on this page (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 159, eqs. 333–335).

### R15

**Scan 159, (332).** **Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium. Reason:** the surviving formulas supply several coordinate presentations whose agreement can be checked independently once the readings are fixed. A useful verification exercise would be to write out the low levels of the coordinate quotient and the chosen representative, recording exactly which sign conventions yield the two written Kummer factors. This is a proposed consistency check, not evidence for changing an uncertain sign or adding the missing exponent in the transcription (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 159).

### R16

**Scan 160, opening paragraph and first display.** **Kind: conjecture / expectation.** “L'hom (329) doit pouvoir s'interpréter en termes de l'hom galoisien” is an explicit expectation of a field-theoretic interpretation involving all roots of −1/2 over the cyclotomic extension. Establishing it would require comparing the tower-fibre action and the action on the relevant radical extension, including the chosen compatible root systems (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 160, opening paragraph and first display).

### R17

**Scan 160, middle paragraph.** **Kind: unpursued implication.** The stated aim is to “apprécier la ‘taille’ de l'image” in the displayed affine profinite group. The subsequent generalization to several radicals suggests an image-size or index problem for Kummer representations, but this page itself supplies no openness or surjectivity conclusion (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 160, middle paragraph).

### R18

**Scan 161, Kummer sequence through (340).** **Kind: unproved claim.** “Par image inverse … une tour d'extensions de schémas en groupes étales sur S” asserts that the maps from A_S yield the displayed extension tower. A full verification would specify the finite-level fibre products and their group laws, then justify passage to the inverse-limit extension in the category being used (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 161, eqs. 338–340).

### R19

**Scan 161, final paragraph and (341).** **Kind: unproved claim.** From connectedness and a geometric point the text obtains “un hom canonique” into automorphisms of the fibre. To formulate this precisely one must identify the class of inverse systems or group objects represented by E∞, particularly because A is initially an arbitrary abelian group rather than an explicitly finite group (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 161, final paragraph and eq. 341).

### R20

**Scan 162, (342)–(345).** **Kind: unproved claim.** “L'hom (341) est compatible avec cette structure d'extension” combines trivial action on A with the cyclotomic action on the kernel, and (345) exhibits a Hom-group as the kernel of a multiplier map. A proof would compare automorphisms of the given abelian extension with maps from its quotient into its kernel, while keeping track of which scalar automorphisms of the kernel lift to automorphisms of the extension (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 162, eqs. 342–345).

### R21

**Scan 162, definition (344) and diagram (345).** **Kind: own idea. Confidence: medium. Reason:** a lift of a scalar automorphism of the kernel must preserve the isomorphism class of the extension, which is the obstruction naturally suggested by (342). One could organize the possible multipliers as the stabilizer of the extension class under the scalar action and compare that stabilizer with the cyclotomic image. This is an observation to check against subsequent manuscript pages and the literature, not a claimed new result or an assertion established by this segment (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 162).

### R22

**Scan 163, remark; unproved claim.** The assertion that the cyclotomic image is an “s-groupe ouvert” for `S` essentially of finite type over `Q` is stated without proof on this page. A proof would need to identify the algebraic constant field and relate its cyclotomic intersection to the image; the exact hypotheses on connectedness and the preceding choice of base point must be retained (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 163).

### R23

**Scan 163, remark; unproved claim.** The phrase “i.e. si … les seules racines de 1 sur S sont ±1” is presented as a criterion for surjectivity of the cyclotomic character. Its precise relationship with the preceding surjectivity claim needs checking independently; it must not be silently strengthened or repaired in the transcription (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 163).

### R24

**Scan 163, remark; own idea — high confidence in the proposed test.** Test the preceding criterion on `S = Spec Q(√2)`: the field is real, so its roots of unity are only ±1, while `√2 = ζ₈ + ζ₈⁻¹` lies in a cyclotomic field, forcing a restriction on the cyclotomic image modulo 8. This is a suggested counterexample check to the literal criterion, subject to verifying that no extra hypothesis from surrounding pages excludes it; no claim of novelty is made (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 163).

### R25

**Scans 163–164, paragraph beginning “Le diagramme”; unproved claim.** Grothendieck calls functoriality in `A` “évidente”. The next page gives the pullback and transposed-Hom diagram, so a formal proof would amount to checking equivariance of that pullback and every arrow rather than supplying an independent construction (Grothendieck, 1981, scans 163–164).

### R26

**Scan 164, right margin; unproved claim.** The annotation “injectif si j est surjectif” states injectivity of the induced automorphism-group map. A proof should use surjectivity of `j` to show that an automorphism of the extension is determined by its pullback, keeping the scalar action on the kernel and identity action on the quotient explicit (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 164).

### R27

**Scan 165, prose after (352); unpursued implication.** The reduction of the “taille” of the full image to the cyclotomic image and the image on the cyclotomic kernel invites a separate study of index and of extension structure. The displayed exact sequence (354) determines the kernel and quotient but does not by itself identify its extension class (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 165).

### R28

**Scan 165, last line, continuing on scan 166; conjecture / expectation.** “Je voudrais essayer de donner des” introduces a proposed investigation of the size of the kernel image. The next page supplies hypotheses, so this is an announced programme rather than a conclusion already proved here (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 165).

### R29

**Scan 166, after (355); unproved claim.** “Les groupes cherchés … ne changent pas” under replacement of a normal base by its generic point is asserted without proof. A proof should compare the relevant étale or Kummer covering images and explain the role of normality and the chosen geometric generic base point (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 166).

### R30

**Scan 166, lower paragraph; unproved claim.** “On peut également supposer … que S est affine, et lisse” announces a further reduction. Its justification should distinguish passing to the generic field from choosing and shrinking a model over `Q`, and check that the image under study is preserved (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 166).

### R31

**Scan 167, after (357); conjecture / expectation.** “J'aurais envie de penser” introduces the expectation that the image denoted `Π̃₁⁺` is open in `Hom_Z(A,T)`. A proof would need control of divisibility in the tower of roots uniformly across the finite levels, under the hypotheses on the preceding page (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R32

**Scan 167, central question; conjecture / question.** “Je me demande … n'est pas nécessairement surjectif??” asks for surjectivity in the case of a normal connected finite-type `Z`-scheme `S₁` with `A = H⁰(S₁,G_m)` and generic fibre `S`. The added dimension condition is not securely read (`2` or `≥ 2`), and the displayed source is literally `Π₁` without `+`; those textual points must be settled before stating a formal theorem (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R33

**Scan 167, final parenthesis; conjecture / expectation.** “Il en est peut être ainsi si … est sans torsion” proposes a more general surjectivity criterion using torsion-freeness of `G_m(S)/Im A`. This should be considered separately from the preceding openness expectation; surjectivity at every finite level is stronger than finite index in the inverse limit (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R34

**Scan 167, final parenthesis; unproved claim.** “On doit pouvoir tjrs se réduire” asserts that one can enlarge `A` to an `A′` inside the units, with `A′/A` finite, to reach the torsion-free-quotient case. A proof must establish finiteness of the saturation of the particular finitely generated subgroup under the hypotheses (355), rather than assume all global units are finitely generated (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R35

**Scan 167, displayed question; own idea — high confidence in the issue to check.** Before interpreting the written map from the full `Π₁` as a group homomorphism to the Tate-twisted additive group, compare it with the cocycle or affine action produced by choosing a point of the earlier torsor. The reason for this check is that the manuscript has previously separated the cyclotomic kernel from the full group; this observation does not justify changing the written source (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R36

**Scan 167, final parenthesis; own idea — medium confidence.** Compare saturation inside the original unit group with divisibility after the cyclotomic base change, since restricting to the cyclotomic kernel may create additional roots. This is a route for testing whether the proposed torsion-free criterion yields exact surjectivity or only openness, not a claim that the criterion is false or novel (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 167).

### R37

**Scan 168, questions a–b; kind: conjecture/question.** He asks « sont-ils surjectifs » and whether the prime-indexed homomorphisms are « linéairement indépendants ». The more precise formulation asks whether the joint map for distinct primes is surjective or at least has open image; these are questions here, not conclusions (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 168).

### R38

**Scan 168, final paragraph; kind: unpursued implication.** The proposed reduction « on décompose … en ses composantes » isolates one prime-adic component at a time. A proof concerning the full profinite image would still have to control simultaneous finite quotients and compatibility across primes, rather than infer the global conclusion merely from separate projection statements (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 168).

### R39

**Scan 168, question b; kind: own idea.** Test the joint-image question on small radical extensions generated by two distinct primes, keeping the cyclotomic base field explicit. **Confidence: medium** as a diagnostic construction, because the finite radical levels directly encode the displayed homomorphisms; this is a suggested check, not a claim that it proves independence (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 168).

### R40

**Scan 169, opening paragraph; kind: conjecture/expectation.** « J'aimerais prouver donc (au moins !) » introduces the wish for surjectivity outside finitely many prime-adic components and an open image in every component. The expectation is preserved at the level asserted; no uniform bound or proof is given on this page (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 169).

### R41

**Scan 169, (364) and concluding sentence; kind: unproved claim.** He asserts the inclusion of the kernel of the local cyclotomic character on inertia into the global cyclotomic kernel, explaining that the other prime-adic characters are trivial on this inertia group. A proof would need to establish precisely that prime-to-local-prime inertia assertion and the compatibility of the embeddings; his parenthesis explicitly warns that the same triviality need not hold on the whole decomposition group (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 169).

### R42

**Scan 170, (365) and final sentence; kind: conjecture/question.** He asks whether the restriction to the cyclotomic kernel inside the local inertia group is « surjectif » or at least has open image. This is a sharper local question than the preceding global-image question, and the page provides no answer (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 170).

### R43

**Scan 170, (365); kind: own idea.** Track the image on each finite level of the displayed local restriction and compare the indices as the exponent grows. **Confidence: medium** as a test strategy, since eventual bounded index would be relevant to the open-image question, but the calculation and any resulting bound are not supplied here (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 170).

### R44

**Scan 171, opening paragraph and (366); kind: unproved claim / formulation.** « Le “théorème de Fermat” peut s'énoncer » introduces the claim that the stated affine curve has no rational point for the displayed range of exponents. The all-plus equation and the invertibility conditions are retained exactly as visible; no conventional sign has been substituted (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 171).

### R45

**Scan 171, (367)–(368); kind: unproved claim.** He identifies the Fermat curve as the inverse image of the displayed `U_1` under the Kummer isogeny. Verification would require checking the quotient by simultaneous scaling and the equation's pullback, including the role of the three nonzero coordinates (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 171).

### R46

**Scan 171, final paragraph; kind: unproved claim / work announced.** « Calculons le genre » begins a calculation using the covering of displayed degree `n²`, with ramification over the stated three points and an étale complement. The excerpt stops when it invokes Hurwitz, so neither the completed computation nor any subsequent qualification is present here (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 171).

### R47

**Scan 171, (366); kind: own idea.** Check the asserted relation to the usual Fermat equation with special attention to even exponents: the literal all-plus homogeneous equation has no nonzero real solution when the exponent is even. **Confidence: high** for this elementary sign observation, because each summand is then nonnegative; it is a reason to investigate the author's convention, not a license to alter the transcription or to assert what another page says (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 171).

### R48

**Scan 171, final paragraph; kind: own idea.** Reconstruct the proposed genus calculation separately from the transcription, keeping the covering degree, the number of points over each branch value, and the ramification indices explicit. **Confidence: high** as a verification task, because the text itself supplies those inputs or indicates where they are intended, while the numerical conclusion is absent from this page (Grothendieck, 1981, scan 171).

## 7. Technical and coverage note

The fragment contains 19 scan markers and 39 tikz-cd diagrams. The inventory contains 292 element entries, each marked `yes`, and uses the seven requested element categories. No non-diagram pictorial drawing was identified in these scans. Handwritten relation diagrams and exact sequences are represented as diagrams; uncertain objects and arrow labels retain their local doubt marks.

The `.tex` file is intentionally a fragment without a preamble, document environment, or definitions of the editorial macros. It is intended for an enclosing LaTeX document providing the requested macros and standard mathematical typesetting support (`amsmath`, `amssymb`, `graphicx`, and `tikz-cd`). The `\diagram` macro must accept mathematical content. Each tikz-cd uses `ampersand replacement=\&` so its matrix can be passed through that macro. The manuscript’s underline markers are preserved as `\uline` / `\uuline`; the enclosing document should give these suitable text and math definitions. An enclosing test document passed a draft-mode LaTeX syntax compilation across all 19 scans, without undefined commands, missing characters, or box warnings; this is a syntax check, not a guarantee of the uncertain readings.

## Reference

Grothendieck, A. (1981). *La Longue Marche à travers la théorie de Galois* [Handwritten manuscript, cote 140-4; supplied filename scans 153–171, from the archive file covering pages 650bis–787]. Université de Montpellier.

## Scope, provenance, and reading conventions

This package transcribes all 19 supplied scan images, using their filename numbers 172–190. It is a diplomatic reading of the attached rasters: uncertainty marks are substantive editorial information, and a completed inventory entry means that the element is represented, not that every character is certain. No external transcription, published edition, or later mathematical result was used to repair the manuscript.

The printed archive counters are one less than the filename numbers. Original large manuscript foliation is retained with `\mspage`. The institutional footer, its printed counter, and the matching pale pencilled archive foliation are archival apparatus and are excluded from the manuscript body. No manuscript page in this set is blank. The first and last images both contain text continuing across the supplied boundary.
